Constructing linear bicategories
Richard Blute Rose Kudzman-Blais* Department of Mathematics and Statistics University of Ottawa Ottawa, Ontario, Canada Susan Niefield Department of Mathematics Union College Schenectady, NY, USA

Abstract
Linearly distributive categories were introduced to model the tensor/par fragment of linear logic, without resorting to the use of negation. Linear bicategories are the bicategorical version of linearly distributive categories. Essentially, a linear bicategory has two forms of composition, each determining the structure of a bicategory, and the two compositions are related by a linear distribution. After the initial paper on the subject, there was little further work as there seemed to be a lack of examples. The main goal of this paper is to demonstrate that there are in fact a great many examples, which are obtained by considering quantales and quantaloids, and by extending familiar constructions from the (ordinary) bicategorical setting. While it is standard in the field of monoidal topology that the category of quantale-valued relations is a bicategory, we begin by showing that if (and only if) the quantale is Girard , we obtain a Girard quantaloid, which is a linear bicategory. The tropical and arctic semiring structures fit together into a Girard quantale, so this construction is likely to have multiple applications. More generally, we define LD -quantales, which are sup-lattices with two quantale structures related by a linear distribution, and their bicategorical analogue, linear quantaloids. We show that Q Q - Rel is a linear quantaloid if and only if Q Q is an LD -quantale. We then consider several standard constructions from enriched bicategory theory, and show that these lift to the linear quantaloid setting and produce new examples of linear bicategories. In particular, we consider linear 𝒬 {\cal Q} -categories, matrices in 𝒬 {\cal Q} and linear monads in 𝒬 {\cal Q} , where 𝒬 {\cal Q} is a linear quantaloid. We develop non-locally posetal examples as well. We consider 𝒬 ​ uant {\cal Q}{\rm uant} , the bicategory of quantales, modules and module homomorphisms, and 𝒬 ​ tld {\cal Q}{\rm tld} , the bicategory of quantaloids, modules and module homomorphisms. These turn out to be cyclic ∗ * -autonomous bicategories, which are in essence a closed version of linear bicategories.



PRELIMINARY DRAFT-NOT FOR DISTRIBUTION

1
Introduction

The idea behind the theory of linearly distributive categories (LDCs) as introduced by Cockett and Seely
[
7
]
is that (the multiplicative fragment of) linear logic is best modelled by taking the two multiplicative connectives,
\otimes
(tensor) and
\oplus
(par), as primitive. One then obtains a category with two monoidal structures, related by
linear distributions
. A linear distribution is a natural transformation, not an isomorphism typically, of the following form or a symmetric equivalent:

A\otimes(B\oplus C)\rightarrow(A\otimes B)\oplus C

This approach to the model theory of linear logic differs from the original approach, using the
*
-autonomous categories of Barr
[
4
]
, of taking tensor and negation as primitive and then defining the par by de Morgan duality.

Just as monoidal categories can be viewed as one-object bicategories, one can ask for the bicategorical version of linearly distributive categories. These are the
linear bicategories
of Cockett, Koslowski and Seely
[
9
]
, which provide a natural semantics for non-commutative linear logic. The primary goal of this paper is to give new classes of linear bicategories arising from several different sources.

Before diving into the formal definitions, we describe the example that led to the more general discussion below. The structures we consider are two ordered semiring
[
15
]
structures on the extended integers
\mbox{$\mathbb{Z}_{\infty}$}=\mathbb{Z}\cup\{+\infty,-\infty\}
. (One could just as well consider the extended reals.) This set in fact has two semiring structures and these are typically called the
tropical
and
arctic
semirings. They are of great use in the theory of synchronization as considered in
[
2
]
. See
[
12
,
13
]
for how extensively these structures arise.

In both, multiplication is given by the usual addition of integers. But we must be careful in defining
\infty+-\infty
. In the first structure, we define
-\infty+_{1}\infty=-\infty=\infty+_{1}-\infty
. The addition for this structure is given by
max
. This gives
\mathbb{Z}_{\infty}
the structure of an ordered semiring with
\mathbb{Z}_{\infty}
equipped with its usual order.

For the second structure, we again have that the multiplication is given by addition. But analogously we now must have
-\infty+_{2}\infty=\infty=\infty+_{2}-\infty
. The addition for this structure is given by
min
. This gives
\mathbb{Z}_{\infty}
the structure of an ordered semiring with
\mathbb{Z}_{\infty}
equipped with the opposite of its usual order.

If
X
and
Y
are sets, we will define a
\mathbb{Z}_{\infty}
-
relation
from
X
to
Y
to be a function
R\colon X\times Y\rightarrow\mbox{$\mathbb{Z}_{\infty}$}
. As in the
category of relations, we consider this as a morphism
R\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y
. Then the two semiring constructions above allow us to define two distinct relational compositions.

Explicitly, given

X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle A}}{{\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}}}Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle B}}{{\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}}}Z

we define

A\otimes B(x,z)=\bigvee_{y\in Y}(A(x,y)+_{1}B(y,z))\mbox{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,}A\oplus B(x,z)=\bigwedge_{y\in Y}(A(x,y)+_{2}B(y,z))

We are of course using the fact that both
\mathbb{Z}_{\infty}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{\infty}^{op}
are not just semirings with the above structures, but are in
fact
quantales
. We are thus following the program of
monoidal topology
as described in
[
17
]
.

The current project began with the observation that the two compositions described above are related by a
linear distribution
and in fact determine a locally posetal linear bicategory. On the other hand, we have the observation that
\mathbb{Z}_{\infty}
with the above operations is a Girard quantale
[
32
,
27
]
and the two structures are related by the Girard duality.

We first introduce the quantale analogue of linearly distributive categories, and the quantaloidal analogue of linear bicategories, in Section
3
, which we call
LD
-quantales
and
linear quantaloids
respectively. These definitions will underlie most of our main results. All Girard quantales are
LD
-quantales and all Girard quantaloids are linear quantaloids.

Our first result in Section
4
is that the category
Q
-
Rel
is a Girard quantaloid
[
26
]
, and therefore a locally posetal linear bicategory, if and only if
Q
is a Girard quantale. This extends naturally to the case where
Q
is an arbitrary
LD
-quantale. We then provide concrete examples of
Q
-
Rel
as a linear quantaloid.

In Section
5
, following the general theory of enriching in a bicategory and the work of Rosenthal
[
26
]
, we introduce the quantaloid
{\cal Q}
-
Mod
whose 0-cells are
{\cal Q}
-categories, 1-cells are
{\cal Q}
-modules and 2-cells are pointwise inequalities, where
{\cal Q}
is a Girard quantaloid.
{\cal Q}
-
Mod
is then itself a Girard quantaloid. This leads to considering enrichment in a linear quantaloid
{\cal Q}
and the introduction the bicategory
{\cal Q}
-
Mod
of linear
{\cal Q}
-categories and linear
{\cal Q}
-modules. It is shown to be a linear quantaloid if and only if
{\cal Q}
is itself linear. This result proceeds by first proving the corresponding theorems for linear monads in
{\cal Q}
and matrices in
{\cal Q}
. Given these new constructions, we provide more examples of locally posetal linear bicategories, using the linear quantaloids presented in the previous section.

We finally develop non-locally posetal examples as well. This is done in Section
6
. We begin by considering
{\cal L}{\rm oc}
, the bicategory whose objects are locales, 1-cells are bimodules and two-cells are bimodule homomorphisms, which we use to illustrate a more general notion. This turns out to be what the authors of
[
9
]
refer to as cyclic
*
-autonomous bicategories, which are linear bicategories. We show that a number of classic examples of bicategories fit into this framework. In particular, the bicategories of quantales and of quantaloids (with their respective modules) are linear bicategories.

Remark 1.1
.

There are unfortunate notational conflicts between linear logic notation and the usual notation for ordered structures, as well as within the linear logic community. We now give our choice for notation for the remainder of the paper, chosen to be in line with the notation of
[
11
]
and
[
9
]
.

•

For partially ordered sets, we will denote the top element by
1
and the bottom element by
0
, if they exist.

•

We will denote quantales by
Q
and quantaloids by
{\cal Q}
.

•

Composition of arrows will be written with diagrammatic ordering.

•

In a monoidal category, we will use
\otimes
to denote the tensor product and
\top
to denote the unit, including in the case of quantales (as opposed to
\&
and
1
used in
[
27
]
). Moreover, we will use
\otimes
to denote composition in a bicategory and
\top_{X}
or
\mbox{$\top\!\!\!\top$}_{X}
to denote the identity 1-cell on
X
, in particular for quantaloids, (as opposed to
\circ
and
i_{X}
used in
[
28
]
).

•

In a
*
-autonomous or linearly distributive category, there is a second monoidal structure, which we will denote by
\oplus
(rather than the
\bindnasrepma
of
[
14
]
). This includes in the case of Girard quantales (as opposed to
\sqcup
and
d
in
[
27
]
) and
LD
-quantales. The unit of this second monoidal product will be denoted by
\perp
.
\oplus
will also denote the second composition in the context of linear bicategories, in particular in Girard or linear quantaloids, and
\bot_{X}
or
\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{X}
will be the identity 1-cell on
X
. Note that
\bot
,
\bot_{X}
and
\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{X}
will also be used to denoted cyclic dualizing elements and families in Girard quantales and quantaloids.

2
Preliminaries

2.1
Quantales and quantaloids

See
[
23
,
27
,
28
]
for a detailed discussion about quantales and quantaloids.

Definition 2.1
.

•

A quantale (coined by Mulvey
[
22
]
) is a partially ordered set
Q
with all suprema and an associative multiplication
\otimes\colon Q\times Q\rightarrow Q
such that for all subsets
P\subseteq Q
and all elements
a\in Q
, we have

\big(\bigvee P\big)\otimes a=\bigvee_{p\in P}p\otimes a\mbox{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, and \,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,}a\otimes\big(\bigvee P\big)=\bigvee_{p\in P}a\otimes p

Note that
Q
necessarily satisfies
a\otimes{\mbox{\bf{0}}=\mbox{\bf{0}}=\mbox{\bf{0}}}\otimes a
.

•

Since the operations
(-)\otimes a
and
a\otimes(-)
preserve all sups, they have right adjoints for all
a\in Q
, known as the left and right residuations. We denote them by
(-)\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}a
and
a\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}(-)
respectively and they are defined by:

c\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}a=\bigvee\{b\in Q\,|\,b\otimes a\leq c\}\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad a\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,c=\bigvee\{b\in Q\,|\,a\otimes b\leq c\}

•

An element
\top\in Q
is a unit if for all
a\in Q,\top\otimes a=a\otimes\top
, in which case
Q
is called unital.

The following definitions and result are due to Yetter
[
32
]
:

•

An element
\bot\in Q
is a cyclic dualizing element if for all
a\in Q
, we have

\bot\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}a=a\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,\bot\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad(a\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,\bot)\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,\bot=a

A Girard quantale is a pair
(Q,\bot)
where
Q
is a quantale and
\bot
is a chosen
cyclic dualizing element. We denote
a\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,\bot
as
a^{\perp}
.

•

If
Q
is a Girard quantale, it has a second multiplication defined by the linear logic version of de Morgan duality:

Lemma 2.2
.

Let
(Q,\bot)
be a Girard quantale, then it is unital with
\top=\bot^{\perp}
and the operation
(-)^{\perp}
is a contravariant isomorphism.
Q^{op}
is thus a unital quantale with multiplication

a\oplus b=(b^{\perp}\otimes a^{\perp})^{\perp}

and unit
\bot
. Evidently this operation satisfies:

(\bigwedge P)\oplus a=\bigwedge_{p\in P}p\oplus a\mbox{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, and \,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,}a\oplus(\bigwedge P)=\bigwedge_{p\in P}a\oplus p

Example 2.3
.

(i)

Every locale (or frame)
L
, i.e., complete lattice satisfying the infinite distributive law,
a\wedge(\bigvee b_{i})=\bigvee(a\wedge b_{i})~\forall a,b_{i}\in L
, is a unital quantale with
\otimes=\wedge
and
\top=1
. As discussed in
[
23
]
, a quantale is a locale if and only if it is commutative, right-sided and idempotent. Following is a list of important locales we will consider in this paper:

(a)

Truth value two-chain
\Omega=\{\mbox{\bf{0}},\mbox{\bf{1}}\}

(b)

Totally ordered 3-chain
3=\{\mbox{\bf{0}},1/2,\mbox{\bf{1}}\}
, with residuations given by

c\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}a=\begin{cases}\mbox{\bf{1}}&\mbox{if }a\leq c\\
c&\mbox{if }a>c\end{cases}

(c)

Extended real half-line with opposite ordering
{\sf P_{max}}=([0,\infty]^{op},{\rm max},0)
, with residuations defined in
[
20
]
by

c\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}a=\begin{cases}c&\mbox{if }a<c\\
0&\mbox{if }a\geq c\end{cases}

(d)

Lattice of open sets
{\cal O}(X)
in a topological space
X

(ii)

The set of relations
\mbox{$\sf Rel$}(X)
on a set
X
is a unital quantale with standard relational composition as its operation and with the diagonal relation
\Delta_{X}
as its unit, i.e., given relations
R,S\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}X
,

(x,x^{\prime\prime})\in R\otimes S\quad\mbox{if and only if}\quad\exists x^{\prime}\mbox{\,\,\,\,\,}(x,x^{\prime})\in R\,\,\mbox{\,and\,}\,\,(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})\in S

(iii)

The extended real half-line with opposite ordering can be equipped with other quantale structures. In particular, consider
{\sf P_{+}}=([0,\infty]^{op},+,0)
, with its operation standard addition extended by
a+\infty=\infty+a=\infty
. It is often called Lawvere’s quantale of positive real numbers, as it was first introduced by Lawvere in
[
20
]
. Residuation is given by “truncated subtraction”:

c\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}a=\begin{cases}c-a&\mbox{if }a\leq c<\infty\\
0&\mbox{if }a\geq c\\
\infty&\mbox{if }a<c=\infty\end{cases}

(iv)

The unit interval with multiplication
([0,1],\cdot,1)
is isomorphic to
{\sf P_{+}}=([0,\infty]^{op},+,0)
under the map
x\mapsto-{\sf ln}(x)
, a unital homomorphism of quantales (function preserving arbitrary sups, quantale operation
\otimes
and the unit
\top
) with inverse
y\mapsto{\sf exp}(-y)
. Residuations are given “truncated division”, as defined in
[
16
]
:

c\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}a=\begin{cases}c/a&\mbox{if }0\neq a>c\\
1&\mbox{otherwise }\end{cases}

Definition 2.4
.

•

A quantaloid (coined by Abramsky and Vickers
[
1
]
) is a category
{\cal Q}
enriched over the category of complete
lattices and sup-preserving maps.

•

As in the case of quantales, for all arrows
f\colon a\rightarrow b\in\mbox{${\cal Q}$}
, the functors
(-)\otimes f\colon\mbox{${\cal Q}$}(a^{\prime},a)\rightarrow\mbox{${\cal Q}$}(a^{\prime},b)
and
f\otimes(-)\colon\mbox{${\cal Q}$}(b,b^{\prime})\rightarrow\mbox{${\cal Q}$}(a,b^{\prime})
preserve all sups and thus have right adjoints, also known as residuations, denoted by
(-)\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}f\colon\mbox{${\cal Q}$}(a^{\prime},b)\rightarrow\mbox{${\cal Q}$}(a^{\prime},a)
and
f\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}(-)\colon\mbox{${\cal Q}$}(a,b^{\prime})\rightarrow\mbox{${\cal Q}$}(b,b^{\prime})
respectively.

The following definitions and result are due to Rosenthal
[
26
]
:

•

A family of 1-cells
\mbox{${\cal D}$\,\,}=\{\bot_{a}\colon a\rightarrow a\mid a\in\mbox{${\cal Q}$}\}
is a cyclic family if
f\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}~\bot_{a}=\bot_{b}~\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}f
, for all
f\colon a\rightarrow b
, and let
f^{\perp}
denote their common value. Then
{\cal D}

is called a cyclic dualizing family if
f^{\perp\perp}=f
, for all
f
.

A Girard quantaloid is a quantaloid
{\cal Q}
together with a cyclic dualizing family
{\cal D}

​.

•

Mirroring Lemma
2.2
, if
{\cal Q}
is Girard, it has a second composition
\oplus
given by the linear logic version of the Morgan duality, such that
\mbox{${\cal Q}$}^{co}
is also a quantaloid (where
-^{co}
denotes the reversal of 2-cells).

Remark 2.5
.

Quickly, we introduce a bit of notation that will be used throughout this paper. Suppose
(\mbox{${\cal V}$},\otimes,\top)
is a monoidal category then let
\cal B(\mbox{${\cal V}$})
denote its suspension, i.e., the bicategory with one object
\star
whose 1-cells and 2-cells are the objects and morphisms of
{\cal V}
, respectively, with composition given by the tensor product
\otimes
and
\rm\top_{\star}
given by the unit
\top
of
\cal V
.

The next preliminary subsections will outline constructions that give rise to various examples of quantaloids, but we outline here three key examples.

Example 2.6
.

(i)

{\cal B}(Q)
, the suspension of a unital quantale, is a quantaloid with one object. Note that a Girard quantaloid with one object is a Girard quantale.

(ii)

\sf Rel
, the locally posetal bicategory of sets and relations, is a quantaloid with hom-sets ordered under inclusion and standard relational composition: if we have
R\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y
and
S\colon Y\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Z
then define

(x,z)\in R\otimes S\quad\mbox{if and only if}\quad\exists y\mbox{\,\,\,\,\,}(x,y)\in R\,\,\mbox{\,and\,}\,\,(y,z)\in S

(iii)

\sf Ord
, the locally posetal bicategory of preordered sets and ideals, is a quantaloid with the standard relational composition: recall that a preordered set is a set
X
endowed with a reflexive and transitive relation
\leq_{X}
and ideals are relations
R\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y
such that

x^{\prime}\leq_{X}x\quad{\rm and}\quad x^{\prime}Ry^{\prime}\quad{\rm and}\quad y^{\prime}\leq_{Y}y\quad\implies\quad xRy

Definition 2.7

(Def 2.2
[
25
]
)
.

If
{\cal Q}
and
\mbox{${\cal Q}$}^{\prime}
are quantaloids, then a quantaloid homomorphism is a functor
F\colon\mbox{${\cal Q}$}\rightarrow\mbox{${\cal Q}$}^{\prime}
such that on hom-sets it induces a sup-lattice morphism
\mbox{${\cal Q}$}(a,b)\rightarrow\mbox{${\cal Q}$}^{\prime}(F(a),F(b))
for all
a,b\in\mbox{${\cal Q}$}
.

2.1.1
The category
Q
-
Rel

A relation
R:X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y
assigns a truth value to each pair in
X\times Y
, as such it can be understood as a function from
X\times Y
to the two-chain quantale.
\sf Rel
can thus be generalized to arbitrary quantales by considering quantale-valued relations as follows, giving rise to a multitude of quantaloid examples.

Definition 2.8
.

If
Q
is a quantale, we can form the category
Q
-
Rel
whose objects are sets and arrows

R\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y
are functions
R\colon X\times Y\rightarrow Q
, called
Q
-relations. Given
R\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y

and
S\colon Y\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Z
, the composition
R\otimes S:X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Z
is defined by

R\otimes S(x,z)=\bigvee_{y\in Y}R(x,y)\otimes S(y,z)

Note that the use of
\otimes
on the left refers to composition in
Q
-
Rel
and on the right refers to multiplication in
Q
. Identities are given by

\top_{X}(x,x^{\prime})=\begin{cases}\top&\mbox{if }x=x^{\prime}\\
\mbox{\bf{0}}&\mbox{if }x\neq x^{\prime}\end{cases}

Lemma 2.9

(
[
17
]
Section 3, 1.1)
.

If
Q
is a unital quantale, then
Q
-
Rel
is a quantaloid under pointwise ordering, so in particular it is a locally posetal bicategory.

As
Q
-
Rel
is a quantaloid, given a Q-relation
R\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y
, there exists residuation functors
(-)\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}R
and
R\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}(-)
, defined for
T\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Z
and
U\colon W\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y
by

\displaystyle R\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,T(y,z)=\bigwedge_{x\in X}R(x,y)\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,T(x,z)

\displaystyle U\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}R(w,z)=\bigwedge_{y\in Y}U(w,y)\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}R(x,y)

Example 2.10
.

(i)

\mbox{$\sf Rel$}\cong 2
-
Rel
, the quantaloid of sets and relations.

(ii)

{\sf P_{+}}
-
Rel
, a quantaloid of sets and extended distance relations
d\colon X\times Y\rightarrow[0,\infty]
, with composition
\otimes
defined, for
D_{1}\colon X\times Y\rightarrow[0,\infty]
and
D_{2}\colon Y\times Z\rightarrow[0,\infty]
, by

(D_{1}\otimes D_{2})(x,z)=\bigwedge_{y\in Y}D_{1}(x,y)+D_{2}(y,z)

and identities
\top_{X}
defined by

\top_{X}(x,x^{\prime})=\begin{cases}0&\mbox{if }x=x^{\prime}\\
\infty&\mbox{if }x\neq x^{\prime}\end{cases}

Alternatively, one can consider
[0,1]
-
{\sf Rel}
, isomorphic to
{\sf P_{+}}
-
Rel
as the map
[0,1]\cong{\sf P_{+}}
extends to an isomorphism of quantaloids, with composition
\otimes
defined, for
D_{1}\colon X\times Y\rightarrow[0,1]
and
D_{2}\colon Y\times Z\rightarrow[0,1]
, by

(D_{1}\otimes D_{2})(x,z)=\bigvee_{y\in Y}D_{1}(x,y)\cdot D_{2}(y,z)

and identities
\top_{X}
defined by

\top_{X}(x,x^{\prime})=\begin{cases}1&\mbox{if }x=x^{\prime}\\
0&\mbox{if }x\neq x^{\prime}\end{cases}

(iii)

{\sf P_{max}}
-
Rel
, another quantaloid of sets and extended distance relations
d\colon X\times Y\rightarrow[0,\infty]
, but with a different composition
\otimes
defined, for
D_{1}\colon X\times Y\rightarrow[0,\infty]
and
D_{2}\colon Y\times Z\rightarrow[0,\infty]
, by

(D_{1}\otimes D_{2})(x,z)=\bigwedge_{y\in Y}{\sf max}(D_{1}(x,y),D_{2}(y,z))

The last two examples will connect to Lawvere metric spaces. This will be discussed in
Example
2.31
.

Note that there is a quantaloid embedding (quantaloid homomorphism which is injective on objects and a faithful functor) from the suspension of the quantale
{\cal B}(Q)
into
Q
-
Rel
:

{\cal B}(Q)\hookrightarrow\mbox{$Q$-{\sf Rel}}:\quad\quad\star\mapsto 1=\{*\}\quad\quad a\mapsto R_{a}\colon 1\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}1\quad{\rm where}\quad R_{a}(*,*)=a

2.2
Modules, matrices and monads

We assume the reader is familiar with the theory of bicategories, introduced by Benabou. Appropriate references are
[
5
,
21
]
. In this section, we introduce three standard constructions in bicategory theory, but first we must review the notion of a biclosed bicategory.

2.2.1
Biclosed bicategories

Recall the definitions of right extensions and right liftings (in the sense of Street
[
30
]
).

Definition 2.11
.

Let
\cal B
be a bicategory. A right extension of
B\colon X\rightarrow Z
along
A\colon X\rightarrow Y
in
{\cal B}
is a 1-cell which we denote
A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}~B
, also
denoted by
X{\rm Mod}(A,B)
, together with a 2-cell

\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{Y}
\scriptstyle{A}
\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{Z}
\scriptstyle{B}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{Z}
\scriptstyle{A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}B}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon_{X,A}}

inducing a bijection (called the transpose) between 2-cells
C\rightarrow A\,\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}B
and
A\otimes C\rightarrow B
, for all
C\colon Y\rightarrow Z
.

A right lifting of
C\colon Z\rightarrow Y
through
A\colon X\rightarrow Y
is a right extension in
{\cal B}^{op}
, i.e., a 1-cell which we denote
C~\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A
, also denoted by
{\rm Mod}Y(A,C)
, together with a 2-cell

\textstyle{Z}
\textstyle{Y}
\scriptstyle{C}
\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{Z}
\scriptstyle{C\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A}
\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{Y}
\scriptstyle{A}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon_{C,Y}}

inducing a bijection (called the transpose) between 2-cells
B\rightarrow C\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A
and
B\otimes A\rightarrow C
, for all
B\colon Z\rightarrow X
.

A bicategory
\cal B
is called biclosed if it admits all right extensions and right liftings.

Remark 2.12
.

We have chosen to use the more traditional notation and terminology from bicategory theory. The authors of

[
9
]
chose their terminology to be in agreement with Lambek’s non-commutative linear logic rather than the theory of
bicategories. We note that our notion of right extension is the same as their notion of right hom and our notion of right lifting
is their left hom.

Example 2.13
.

Suppose
(\mbox{${\cal V}$},\otimes,\top)
is a symmetric monoidal closed category and consider its suspension
\cal B(\mbox{${\cal V}$})
. Taking
A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}~B={\rm Hom}(A,B)
and
C\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A={\rm Hom}(A,C)
, we get:
\cal B(\mbox{${\cal V}$})
is a biclosed bicategory.

Proposition 2.14
.

If
{\cal B}
is a biclosed bicategory, then the induced 2-cells

(A\otimes C)\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,B\rightarrow C\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,(A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,B)\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad D\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}(A\otimes C)\rightarrow(D\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}C)\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A

are invertible and natural in
A\colon X\rightarrow Y
,
B\colon X\rightarrow W
,
C\colon Y\rightarrow Z
and
D\colon W\rightarrow Z
.

Proof.

This follows from the universal properties of the extensions and liftings.
∎

2.2.2
{\cal B}
-categories and modules

The following definition was first introduced for locally posetal bicategories by Walters
[
31
]
and then defined for more general bicategories by Street
[
29
]
.

Definition 2.15
.

Let
{\cal B}
be a biclosed bicategory.

•

A
{\cal B}
-category
M
consists of the following data:

–

for each 0-cell
A\in\mbox{${\cal B}$}
, a set
M_{A}
“over
A
”,

–

for each pair of elements
x,x^{\prime}
over 0-cells
A,B
respectively, a 1-cell
M(x,x^{\prime})\colon A\rightarrow B
in
{\cal B}
,

–

for each triple of elements
x,x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}
over 0-cells
A,B,C
respectively, 2-cells
\eta\colon 1_{A}\rightarrow M(x,x)
and
\mu\colon M(x,x^{\prime})\otimes M(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})\rightarrow M(x,x^{\prime\prime})
in
{\cal B}

satisfying the axioms of left and right identities, and associativity.

Alternatively, a
{\cal B}
-category is lax functor from the union of sets
M_{A}
viewed as an indiscrete bicategory to
{\cal B}
.

•

A
{\cal B}
-module
\Theta\colon M\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}N
assigns:

–

to each pair
x\in M_{A}
and
y\in N_{B}
over 0-cells
A
and
B
, a 1-cell
\Theta(x,y)\colon A\rightarrow B
in
{\cal B}
,

–

to each triple
x,x^{\prime}\in M_{A}
and
y\in N_{B}
over 0-cells
A
and
B
, a left action 2-cell
\rho\colon M(x,x^{\prime})\otimes\Theta(x^{\prime},y)\rightarrow\Theta(x,y)
in
{\cal B}
,

–

to each triple
x\in M_{A}
and
y,y^{\prime}\in N_{B}
over 0-cells
A
and
B
, a right action 2-cell
\lambda\colon\Theta(x,y)\otimes N(y,y^{\prime})\rightarrow\Theta(x,y^{\prime})
in
{\cal B}
,

satisfying five compatibility axioms.

•

Given
{\cal B}
-modules
\Theta,\Phi\colon M\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}N
, a morphism of
{\cal B}
-modules
\alpha\colon\Theta\rightarrow\Phi
is a family of 2-cells
\alpha\colon\Theta(x,y)\rightarrow\Phi(x,y)
in
{\cal B}
compatible with the left and right actions
\lambda,\phi
.

•

Given
{\cal B}
-modules
\Theta\colon M\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}N
and
\Pi:N\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}P
, the composite
\Theta\otimes\Pi\colon M\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}N
is defined, for
x\in M_{A}
and
z\in P_{C}
over
A
and
C
, by
\Theta\otimes\Pi(x,z)
as a colimit in
\mbox{${\cal B}$}(A,C)
.

•

By restricting to certain bicategories
{\cal B}
, we can define the bicategory
{\cal B}
-
Mod
of
{\cal B}
-categories and
{\cal B}
-modules.

For more details, see
[
29
]
.

Suppose
(\mbox{${\cal V}$},\otimes,\top)
is a complete and cocomplete symmetric monoidal closed category. Then we can consider
{\cal B(V)}
-
Mod
\,\cong\,
{\cal V}
-
{\cal P}\rm rof
, the bicategory of
{\cal V}
-categories,
{\cal V}
-profunctors (sometimes called
{\cal V}
-distributors), and
{\cal V}
-transformations.

Note that composition
A\otimes_{R}B
of
{\cal V}
-profunctors
A\colon Q\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}R
and
B\colon R\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}S
can be described by the coend

(A\otimes_{R}B)(q,s)=\int^{r}A(q,r)\otimes B(r,s)

and
Q(-,-)\colon Q\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Q
is the identity 1-cell.

2.2.3
{\cal B}
-matrices

The work of categories enriched in bicategories was further developed by Betti, Carboni, Street and Walters
[
6
]
. They introduced the bicategory of
{\cal B}
-matrices as a stepping stone to the study of
{\cal B}
-
Mod
.

Definition 2.16

(
[
6
]
)
.

Let
{\cal B}
be a locally small-cocomplete bicategory with a small set of objects
\mbox{${\cal B}$}_{0}
.

•

Given a family
X=(X_{A})_{A\in{\cal B}_{0}}
of small sets indexed by
\mbox{${\cal B}$}_{0}
, an element
x\in X_{A}
is said to be an element of
X
over
A
.

•

Given a pair of families
X=(X_{A})_{A\in{\cal B}_{0}}
and
Y=(Y_{A})_{A\in{\cal B}_{0}}
, a
{\cal B}
-matrix
S\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y
assigns to each pair
x,y
of elements over
A,B\in\mbox{${\cal B}$}_{0}
a 1-cell
S(x,y)\colon A\rightarrow B
in
{\cal B}
. Composition of
{\cal B}
-matrices
S\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y
and
T\colon Y\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Z
is by matrix multiplication, i.e.,
(S\otimes T)(x,z)=\coprod_{y\in Y}S(x,y)\otimes T(y,z)
.

•

A morphism of
{\cal B}
-matrices
\alpha\colon S\rightarrow S^{\prime}
is a family of 2-cells
\alpha_{x,y}\colon S(x,y)\rightarrow S^{\prime}(x,y)
in
{\cal B}
.

•

Define
Matr
{\cal B}
to be the bicategory with families of small sets indexed by
\mbox{${\cal B}$}_{0}
as 0-cells,
{\cal B}
-matrices as 1-cells and
{\cal B}
-matrix morphisms as 2-cells.

Suppose
(\mbox{${\cal V}$},\otimes,\top)
is a symmetric monoidal closed category with set-indexed products and coproducts. Then, we can consider
Matr
{\cal B(V)}
\,\cong\,
{\cal V}
-
Mat
, the bicategory of sets,
\cal V
-matrices, and
\cal V
-matrix morphisms.

Recall
[
8
]
that a
\cal V
-matrix
A\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y
is a function
A\colon X\times Y\rightarrow{\sf ob}\mbox{${\cal V}$}
. A morphism
f\colon A\rightarrow B
of
\cal V
-matrices
X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y
is a family

\{f(x,y)\colon A(x,y)\rightarrow B(x,y)\mid(x,y)\in X\times Y\}

of morphisms of
\cal V
. Composition of
A\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y
and
B\colon Y\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Z
is given by matrix multiplication

(A\otimes_{Y}B)(x,z)=\coprod_{y\in Y}A(x,y)\otimes B(y,z)

and the identity
X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}X
is

\top_{X}(x,x^{\prime})=\begin{cases}\top&x=x^{\prime}\\
\mbox{\bf{0}}&\text{otherwise}\\
\end{cases}

where
\top
is the unit for
\otimes
and
0
is initial in
\cal V
.

Given
{\cal V}
-matrices
A\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y
,
B\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Z
, and
C\colon Z\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y
, taking

A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,B(y,z)=\prod_{x\in X}\mbox{${\cal V}$}(A(x,y),B(x,z))\qquad C\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A(z,x)=\prod_{y\in Y}\mbox{${\cal V}$}(A(x,y),C(z,y))

we get the well-known result within folklore (
[
6
]
and
[
19
]
):

Lemma 2.17
.

If
{\cal V}
is a symmetric monoidal closed category with set-indexed products and coproducts, then
{\cal V}
-
Mat
is a biclosed bicategory.

2.2.4
Monads in
{\cal B}
and modules

Carboni, Kasangian and Walters
[
8
]
demonstrated that
{\cal B}
-
Mod
can be broken into the constructions of
Matr
{\cal B}
and of
Mon
{\cal B}
, the bicategory of monads and modules in
{\cal B}
as defined below.

Definition 2.18

(
[
8
]
)
.

Let
{\cal B}
is a biclosed bicategory with local equalizers and coequalizers stable under composition.

•

A monad in
{\cal B}
is a 1-cell
Q\colon X\rightarrow X
together with two 2-cells
e\colon 1_{X}\rightarrow Q
and

m\colon Q\otimes Q\rightarrow Q
satisfying the usual associativity and identity axioms. Note that a monad
(X,Q)
(denoted
by just
Q
when
X
is understood) is a monoid in the category
{\cal B}(X,X)
.

•

A
(Q,R)
-module
A\colon(X,Q)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(Y,R)
is a 1-cell
A\colon X\rightarrow Y
together with action 2-cells
\lambda\colon Q\otimes A\rightarrow A
and
\rho\colon A\otimes R\rightarrow A
satisfying (one-sided) associative and unit laws, and a diagram expressing the commutativity of the two actions.

•

A
(Q,R)
-module morphism is a 2-cell
f\colon A\rightarrow B
satisfying compatibility conditions with respect to the actions.

•

Let
Mon
{\cal B}
denote the bicategory of monads, modules, and module morphisms; with the composition
A\otimes_{R}B
of
A\colon(X,Q)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(Y,R)
and
B\colon(Y,R)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(Z,S)
given by the local coequalizer

A\otimes R\otimes B\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.90707pt\raise 8.57222pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.71112pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rho_{A}\otimes B}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.27676pt\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.67566pt\raise-8.43332pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.92778pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{A\otimes\lambda_{B}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.27676pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>A\otimes B\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>A\otimes_{R}B

in
{\cal B}(X,Z)
, and identity 1-cell
\mbox{$\top\!\!\!\top$}_{(X,Q)}=Q\colon(X,Q)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(X,Q)
.

Note that identity 1-cell
\top_{X}\colon X\rightarrow X
in
{\cal B}
is a trivial monad, every 1-cell
A\colon X\rightarrow Y
is an
(\top_{X},\top_{Y})
-module, and every 2-cell
f\colon A\rightarrow B
is a
(\top_{X},\top_{Y})
-module homomorphism. Moreover,
X\mapsto\top_{X}
defines a pseudo functor which is a left pseudo adjoint to the forgetful (lax) functor
\mbox{{\sf Mon}\mbox{${\cal B}$}}\rightarrow\mbox{${\cal B}$}
.

Given
A\colon(X,Q)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(Y,R)
,
B\colon(X,Q)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(Z,S)
, and
C\colon(Z,S)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(Y,R)
, the right extension
B\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}_{Q}\,A
in
Mon
{\cal B}
of
B
along
A
is obtained by taking the local equalizer.

A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}_{Q}\,B\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.24657pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.24657pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.49313pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,B\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.9809pt\raise 8.67487pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.16931pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\lambda^{*}_{A}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 26.3855pt\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.45573pt\raise-9.86111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.86111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hat{\lambda}_{B}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 26.3855pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(Q\otimes A)\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,B

where
\lambda^{*}_{A}=\lambda_{A}\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,B
and
\hat{\lambda}_{B}
is the transpose of

(Q\otimes A)\otimes(A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,B)\xrightarrow{\sim}Q\otimes(A\otimes(A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,B))\xrightarrow{Q\otimes\epsilon_{X,A}}Q\otimes B\xrightarrow{\lambda_{B}}B

Similarly the right lifting
C\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}_{R}A
in
Mon
{\cal B}
of
C
through
A
is

C\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}_{R}A\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.24657pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.24657pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.49313pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>C\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.27794pt\raise 8.85265pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.99155pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rho^{*}_{A}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 26.3855pt\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.45573pt\raise-9.86111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.86111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hat{\rho}_{B}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 26.3855pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>C\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}(A\otimes R)

Note that given morphisms
A\colon X\rightarrow Y,B\colon X\rightarrow Z
and
C\colon Z\rightarrow Y
in
{\cal B}
the 2-cells

A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}_{\top_{X}}\,B\rightarrow A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,B\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad C\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}_{\top_{Y}}A\rightarrow C\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A

defining the extensions and liftings in
Mon
{\cal B}
, are invertible, and without loss of generality, we can take them to be equalities.

Moreover, given the above definitions, the following is a well-known result (
[
6
]
and
[
19
]
):

Lemma 2.19
.

If
{\cal B}
is a biclosed bicategory with local equalizers and coequalizers stable under composition, then
Mon
{\cal B}
is a biclosed bicategory.

By examining the definitions of the three constructions, it is immediate that:

Proposition 2.20

(
[
8
]
)
.

If
{\cal B}
is a distributive bicategory (locally cocomplete bicategory with colimits preserved by composition on both sides),
{\cal B}
-
Mod
is biequivalent to
Mon
Matr
{\cal B}
.

Furthermore, the constructions are idempotent.

Proposition 2.21

(
[
8
]
)
.

If
{\cal B}
is a distributive bicategory, then
{\cal B}
-
Mod
-
Mod
is biequvilant to
{\cal B}
-
Mod
. If
{\cal B}
is a bicategory with local coequilaziers stable under composition, then
Mon
Mon
{\cal B}
is biequivalent to
Mon
{\cal B}
. If
{\cal B}
is a bicategory with small local coproducts stable under composition, then
Matr
Matr
{\cal B}
is biequivalent to
Matr
{\cal B}
.

Thus, by Lemmas
2.17
and
2.19
, and taking the liftings and extensions by the ends

(A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}B)(r,s)=\int_{q}{\rm Hom}(A(q,r),B(q,s))\qquad(C\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A)(s,q)=\int_{r}{\rm Hom}(A(q,r),B(s,r))

for
{\cal V}
-profunctors
A\colon Q\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}R
,
B\colon Q\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}S
and
C\colon S\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}R
, we get:

Lemma 2.22
.

Suppose
{\cal V}
is a complete and cocomplete symmetric monoidal closed category, then
{\cal V}
-
{\cal P}\rm rof
is a biclosed bicategory.

2.2.5
Enriched in a quantaloid
{\cal Q}

The previous bicategorical constructions can of course be considered in the special case of a quantaloid
{\cal Q}
, as done by Rosenthal
[
26
]
. We take the time to describe them in detail as a main result of this paper is their generalization to the linear context.

Definition 2.23
.

Let
{\cal Q}
be a quantaloid.

•

A
{\cal Q}
-category is a pair
M=(X,\rho)
where:

–

X
is a set,

–

\rho
is a function
\rho\colon X\rightarrow{\sf ob}\mbox{${\cal Q}$}
, and

–

there is a function, called the enrichment assigning to each pair
(x,x^{\prime})\in X\times X
a morphism
M(x,x^{\prime})\colon\rho(x)\rightarrow\rho(x^{\prime})
such that
\forall x,x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\in X

\top_{\rho(x)}\leq M(x,x)\qquad M(x,x^{\prime})\otimes M(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})\leq M(x,x^{\prime\prime})

Alternatively, a
{\cal Q}
-category is a lax functor from set
X
to
{\cal Q}
, when
X
is viewed as an indiscrete bicategory.

•

Consider
{\cal Q}
-categories
M=(X,\rho_{M})
and
N=(Y,\rho_{N})
. A
{\cal Q}
-module
\Theta\colon M\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}N
consists of an assignment of a morphism
\Theta(x,y)\colon\rho_{M}(x)\rightarrow\rho_{N}(y)
to every pair
(x,y)\in X\times Y
such that
\forall x,x^{\prime}\in X,y,y^{\prime}\in Y

\Theta(x,y)\otimes N(y,y^{\prime})\leq\Theta(x,y^{\prime})\qquad M(x,x^{\prime})\otimes\Theta(x^{\prime},y)\leq\Theta(x,y)

•

Define the category
{\cal Q}
-
Mod
whose objects are
{\cal Q}
-categories and arrows are
{\cal Q}
