-modules. Given
M\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Theta}}{{\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}}}N\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Pi}}{{\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}}}P
, composition
\Theta\otimes\Pi\colon M\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}P
is defined by

(\Theta\otimes\Pi)(x,z)=\bigvee_{y\in Y}\Theta(x,y)\otimes\Pi(y,z)

Identity 1-cells
\mbox{$\top\!\!\!\top$}_{M}\colon M\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}M
are defined by
\mbox{$\top\!\!\!\top$}_{M}(x,x^{\prime})=M(x,x^{\prime})
. Note that the use of
\otimes
on the left refers to composition in
{\cal Q}
-
Mod
and on the right refers to composition in
{\cal Q}
.

.

{\cal Q}
-

(

Definition 2.25
.

Let
{\cal Q}
be a quantaloid. Define the category
Matr
{\cal Q}
whose objects are small families of objects in
{\cal Q}
, i.e., pairs
(X,\gamma)
of a set
X
and a function
\gamma\colon X\rightarrow ob\mbox{${\cal Q}$}
, and arrows are
{\cal Q}
-matrices
r\colon(X,\gamma)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(Y,\phi)
, i.e., families of morphisms
(r_{x,y}\colon\gamma(x)\rightarrow\phi(y))_{(x,y)\in X\times Y}
in
{\cal Q}
. Given
{\cal Q}
-matrices
(X,\gamma)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}}}(Y,\phi)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s}}{{\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}}}(Z,\chi)
, composition
r\otimes s\colon(X,\gamma)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(Z,\chi)
is defined by

(r\otimes s)_{x,z}=\bigvee_{y\in Y}r_{x,y}\otimes s_{y,z}

Identity 1-cells
\mbox{$\top\!\!\!\top$}_{(X,\gamma)}\colon(X,\gamma)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(X,\gamma)
are defined by
{\mbox{$\top\!\!\!\top$}_{(X,\gamma)}}_{x,x^{\prime}}=\begin{cases}\top_{\gamma(x)}&\mbox{if }x=x^{\prime}\\
\mbox{\bf{0}}_{\gamma(x),\phi(x^{\prime})}&\mbox{if }x\neq x^{\prime}\end{cases}
.

Example 2.26
.

(i)

It is a standard result that teh category of
{\cal B}(\Omega)
-matrices,
Matr
{\cal B}(\Omega)
, is isomorphic to
\sf Rel
.

(ii)

Consider
Matr
{\cal B}(Q)
. The objects are pairs
(X,\gamma)
of a set
X
and a trivial function mapping the set to
\star
. The
{\cal B}(Q)
-matrices are families
(r_{x,y}\colon\star\rightarrow\star)_{(x,y)\in X\times Y}
, a collections of elements in
Q
, i.e., a function
X\times Y\rightarrow Q
, and composition of
{\cal B}(Q)
-matrices is exactly the one of
Q
-relations. As such,
Matr
{\cal B}(Q)\,\cong\,\mbox{$Q$-{\sf Rel}}
, generalizing the first example.

Definition 2.27
.

Let
{\cal Q}
be a quantaloid.

•

A monad
(a,m)
in
{\cal Q}
is an object
a
equipped with a morphism
m\colon a\rightarrow a
such that

\top_{a}\leq m\qquad m\otimes m\leq m

Alternatively, a monad in
{\cal Q}
is a lax functor from the terminal bicategory 1 to
{\cal Q}
.

•

A
(m,n)
-module
f\colon(a,m)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(b,n)
is a morphism
f\colon a\rightarrow b
in
{\cal Q}
such that

m\otimes f\leq f\qquad f\otimes n\leq f

•

Mon
{\cal Q}
is the category of monads and monad modules. in
{\cal Q}
. Composition is directly inherited from
{\cal Q}
and the identity 1-cell
\mbox{$\top\!\!\!\top$}_{a,m}\colon(a,m)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(a,m)
is
m:a\rightarrow a
.

Example 2.28
.

(i)

It is immediate that monads in
\sf Rel
are preordered sets and monad modules are ideals, thus
Mon
(\mbox{$\sf Rel$})\,\cong\,\mbox{$\sf Ord$}
.

(ii)

The above example can be generalized to
Q
-
Rel
. Consider
Mon
(\mbox{$Q$-{\sf Rel}})
: monads in
Q
-
Rel
are
Q
-categories, pairs
(X,m)
, set
X
equipped with a relation
m\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}X
which is
Q
-reflexive and
Q
-transitive, meaning

\top\leq m(x,x)\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad m(x,x^{\prime})\otimes m(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})\leq m(x,x^{\prime\prime})\quad\quad\forall x,x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\in X

while monad modules are
Q
-profunctors, relations
S\colon(X,m)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(Y,n)

m(x,x^{\prime})\otimes S(x^{\prime},y)\leq S(x,y)\quad{\rm and}\quad S(x,y^{\prime})\otimes n(y^{\prime},y)\leq S(x,y)\quad\forall x,x^{\prime}\in X,y,y^{\prime}\in Y

These constructions are more than just categories:

Lemma 2.29

(
[
26
]
)
.

{\cal Q}
-
Mod
,
Matr
{\cal Q}
and
Mon
{\cal Q}
are quantaloids under pointwise ordering, so in particular they are locally posetal bicategories.

As
{\cal Q}
-
Mod
,
Matr
{\cal Q}
and
Mon
{\cal Q}
are quantaloids, there exist residuation functors. In the case of
{\cal Q}
-
Mod
and
Matr
{\cal Q}
, the formulas are similar to
Q
-
Rel
, the inf of the point-wise residuations, while
Mon
{\cal Q}
inherits resituations directly from
{\cal Q}
.

Propositions
2.20
and
2.21
apply to the case of quantaloids:

Proposition 2.30

(
[
26
]
)
.

{\cal Q}
-
Mod
is biequivalent to
{\sf Mon}\mbox{{\sf Matr}\mbox{${\cal Q}$}}
and the constructions are idempotent.

Thus, we see that
\mbox{$\sf Ord$}\cong\,{\cal B}(\Omega)
-
{\sf Mod}\,\cong\,\mbox{$\sf Rel$}
-
{\sf Mod}
and
Q
-
{\cal P}\rm rof\cong\,{\cal B}(Q)
-
{\sf Mod}\,\cong\,(\mbox{$Q$-{\sf Rel}})
-
{\sf Mod}
.

Example 2.31
.

(i)

Consider
Q={\sf P_{+}}
, then
{\sf P_{+}}
-
{\cal P}\rm rof
is the quantaloid of Lawvere metric spaces and
{\sf P_{+}}
-bimodules (in the sense of
[
20
]
). Objects are sets
X
equipped with extended distance functions
m\colon X\times X\rightarrow[0,\infty]
which satisfy the axiom of point inequality and the triangle inequality:

m(x,x)\leq 0\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad m(x,x^{\prime\prime})\leq m(x,x^{\prime})+m(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})\quad\quad\forall x,x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\in X

In other words the objects are extended quasi-pseudo-metric spaces, or simply Lawvere metric spaces. The arrows
(X,m)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(Y,n)
are real-valued functions
F\colon X\times Y\rightarrow[0,\infty]
satisfying

F(x,y)\leq m(x,x^{\prime})+F(x^{\prime},y)\quad{\rm and}\quad F(x,y)\leq F(x,y^{\prime})+n(y^{\prime},y)\quad\forall x,x^{\prime}\in X,y,y^{\prime}\in Y

The standard category
Met
of Lawvere metric spaces and short maps is equivalent to the category of
{\sf P_{+}}
-categories and
{\sf P_{+}}
-functors. Every
{\sf P_{+}}
-functor gives rise to a pair of adjoint
{\sf P_{+}}
-bimodules, therefore
{\sf P_{+}}
-
{\cal P}\rm rof
is a quantaloid of Lawvere metric spaces with a more general notion of morphisms.

Alternatively, take
Q=[0,1]
, then
[0,1]
-
{\cal P}\rm rof\cong{\sf P_{+}}
-
{\cal P}\rm rof
. Objects are sets
X
equipped with a functions
m\colon X\times X\rightarrow[0,1]
satisfying

1\leq m(x,x)\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad m(x,x^{\prime})\cdot m(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})\leq m(x,x^{\prime\prime})\quad\quad\forall x,x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\in X

while arrows
(X,m)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(Y,n)
are functions
F\colon X\times Y\rightarrow[0,1]
satisfying

m(x,x^{\prime})\cdot F(x^{\prime},y)\leq F(x,y)\quad{\rm and}\quad F(x,y^{\prime})\cdot n(y^{\prime},y\leq f(x,y))\quad\forall x,x^{\prime}\in X,y,y^{\prime}\in Y

(ii)

If instead, we consider
Q={\sf P_{max}}
, then
{\sf P_{max}}
-
{\cal P}\rm rof
is the quantaloid of Lawvere ultrametric spaces and
{\sf P_{max}}
-bimodules. The metric spaces
(X,m)
satisfy the strengthened triangle inequality:

m(x,x^{\prime\prime})\leq{\sf max}(m(x,x^{\prime}),m(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}))\quad\quad\forall x,x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\in X

and the arrows
(X,m)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(Y,n)
are real-valued functions
F\colon X\times Y\rightarrow[0,\infty]
satisfying

F(x,y)\leq{\sf max}(m(x,x^{\prime}),F(x^{\prime},y))\quad{\rm and}\quad F(x,y)\leq{\sf max}(F(x,y^{\prime}),n(y^{\prime},y))\quad\forall x,x^{\prime}\in X,y,y^{\prime}\in Y

For more details, see
[
20
]
.

Note that there is a quantaloid embedding of
{\cal Q}
into
{\cal Q}
-
Mod
,

\mbox{${\cal Q}$}\hookrightarrow\mbox{\mbox{${\cal Q}$}-{\sf Mod}}:\quad\quad a\mapsto M_{a}=(1,\rho_{a})\quad{\rm where}\quad 1={*},\quad\rho_{a}(*)=a\quad{\rm and}\quad M_{a}(*,*)=\top_{a}

f\colon a\rightarrow b\mapsto\Theta_{f}\colon M_{a}\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}M_{b}\quad{\rm where}\quad\Theta_{f}(*,*)=f

of
{\cal Q}
into
Matr
{\cal Q}
,

\mbox{${\cal Q}$}\hookrightarrow\mbox{{\sf Matr}\mbox{${\cal Q}$}}:\quad\quad a\mapsto(1,\gamma_{a})\quad{\rm where}\quad 1={*},\quad\gamma_{a}(*)=a\quad

\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad f\colon a\rightarrow b\mapsto f\colon(1,\gamma_{a})\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(1,\gamma_{b})\quad{\rm where}\quad f_{*,*}=f

and of
{\cal Q}
into
Mon
{\cal Q}
,

\mbox{${\cal Q}$}\hookrightarrow\mbox{{\sf Mon}\mbox{${\cal Q}$}}:\quad\quad a\mapsto(a,\top_{a})~~{\rm the~trivial~monad}

\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad f\colon a\rightarrow b\mapsto f\colon(a,\top_{a})\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(b,\top_{b})

2.3
Linear bicategories

We introduce the theory of
linear bicategories
as defined by Cockett, Koslowski and Seely
[
9
]
. The material in this subsection is entirely from that paper. Linear bicategories are an extension of the theory of
linearly distributive categories
, due to Cockett and Seely
[
7
]
. Linearly distributive categories axiomatize the multiplicative fragment of linear logic in a way that is closer to the syntax. So the two binary connectives,
\otimes
and
\oplus
, are taken as primitives, and negation can be added if one wishes.

As usual with bicategories, one begins with a class of
0-cells
which we will denote
\mbox{${\cal B}$}_{0}=\{X,Y,Z,\ldots\}
. Then for every pair of 0-cells, one has a category
\mbox{${\cal B}$}(X,Y)
. The objects of
\mbox{${\cal B}$}(X,Y)
are called
1-cells
and the arrows are called
2-cells
. But now we have two composition functors:

\otimes,\oplus\colon\mbox{${\cal B}$}(X,Y)\times\mbox{${\cal B}$}(Y,Z)\longrightarrow\mbox{${\cal B}$}(X,Z)

Each of these compositions gives a bicategory structure. Thus for each composition we have all of the morphisms and coherences that this entails. In particular we must have identity 1-cells for each of the two compositions:

\top_{X}\colon X\rightarrow X\mbox{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,and \,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,}\bot_{X}\colon X\rightarrow X

These two bicategory structures are related by a linear distribution as follows. Given 0-cells
X,Y,Z,W
we have two functors:

-\!\!\!-\otimes(-\!\!\!-\oplus-\!\!\!-),\,\,\,\,(-\!\!\!-\otimes-\!\!\!-)\oplus-\!\!\!-:\mbox{${\cal B}$}(X,Y)\times\mbox{${\cal B}$}(Y,Z)\times\mbox{${\cal B}$}(Z,W)-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\rightarrow\mbox{${\cal B}$}(X,W)

and we require a natural transformation between them, which is not necessarily an
isomorphism:

\delta^{L}:-\!\!\!-\otimes(-\!\!\!-\oplus-\!\!\!-)\Longrightarrow(-\!\!\!-\otimes-\!\!\!-)\oplus-\!\!\!-.

Symmetrically, we also need a natural transformation

\delta^{R}:(-\!\!\!-\oplus-\!\!\!-)\otimes-\!\!\!-\Longrightarrow-\!\!\!-\oplus(-\!\!\!-\otimes-\!\!\!-).

All of this structure must satisfy coherence requirements detailed in
[
9
]
.

One of the main goals of this paper will be to provide new examples of linear bicategories, but we mention here the quintessential
example of
\sf Rel
, as it will be the example on which ours are based.

\sf Rel
is a locally posetal bicategory with its first composition being the standard one. But we have a second composition: for
R\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y
and
S\colon Y\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Z
, define

(x,z)\in R\oplus S\mbox{\,\,\,\,\,\, if and only if\,\,\,\,}\forall y\mbox{\,\,\,\,\,\,}(x,y)\in R\,\,\mbox{\, or\,}\,\,(y,z)\in S

2.3.1
Cyclic
*
-autonomous bicategories

The notion of a
*
-autonomous category was originally introduced independently of linear logic in
[
4
]
by M. Barr, who was trying to capture some of the dualities present in various categories of topological vector spaces. It was only later seen to be appropriate to model the multiplicative fragment of linear logic. Barr’s definition of
*
-autonomous category was a symmetric monoidal closed category with a dualizing object. But linear logic suggested an equivalent definition as a linearly distributive category with negation, see
[
7
]
. Similarly, as linearly distributive categories have been generalized to linear bicategories, it is natural to generalize the notion of cyclic
*
-autonomous categories as well.

Definition 2.32

(Def 3.14
[
9
]
)
.

A bicategory
{\cal B}
is cyclic
*
-autonomous bicategory if

•

for any pair of 0-cells
X,Y
, there is an adjoint equivalence
(-)^{*}\dashv((-)^{*})^{op}:\mbox{${\cal B}$}(X,Y)\rightarrow\mbox{${\cal B}$}(Y,X)^{op}
,

•

for any 1-cell
A:X\rightarrow Y
, there is a right extension of
\top_{X}^{*}
along
A
, denoted
A^{*}
, such that these extensions are natural in
A
.

As discussed in
[
9
]
, we can equivalently define cyclic
*
-autonomous bicategory by introducing the concept of dualizing 1-cells.

Definition 2.33
.

Suppose
{\cal D}=\{\bot_{X}\colon X\rightarrow X\,|\,X\in\mbox{${\cal B}$}\}
is a family of 1-cells in a biclosed bicategory
{\cal B}
. Given
A\colon X\rightarrow Y
, applying Definition
2.11
, we get 2-cells

A\,\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.42592pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-0.42592pt\raise 6.41943pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.44167pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\delta_{A,Y}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.99396pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98792pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\,(\bot_{Y}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A)\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\bot_{Y}\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad A\,\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.47107pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-0.47107pt\raise 6.41943pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.44167pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\delta_{X,A}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.99396pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98792pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\,\bot_{X}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}(A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\bot_{X})

•

A family
{\cal D}
is called dualizing if the 2-cells
\delta_{A,Y}
are invertible, for all
A\colon X\rightarrow Y
.

•

A dualizing family
{\cal D}
is called cyclic if there are invertible 2-cells
\theta_{A}\colon\bot_{Y}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A\xrightarrow{\sim}A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\bot_{X}
, natural in
A\colon X\rightarrow Y
, such that the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{A}
\textstyle{\qquad\qquad(\bot_{Y}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A)\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\bot_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{A,Y}}
\textstyle{\qquad\qquad\bot_{X}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}(A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\bot_{X})}
\textstyle{A}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{X,A}}
\textstyle{\qquad\qquad\bot_{X}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}(A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\bot_{X})}
\textstyle{\qquad\qquad(\bot_{Y}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A)\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\bot_{Y}}
\textstyle{\qquad\qquad\bot_{X}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}(A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\bot_{X})}
\textstyle{\qquad\qquad\bot_{X}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}(\bot_{Y}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A)}
\scriptstyle{\bot_{X}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}\theta_{A}}
\textstyle{\qquad\qquad\bot_{X}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}(\bot_{Y}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A)}
\textstyle{\qquad\qquad(\bot_{Y}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A)\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\bot_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{\bot_{Y}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A}}

In this case, we let
A^{\perp}=A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\bot_{X}
.

Proposition 2.34
.

{\cal B}
is a cyclic
*
-autonomous bicategory if and only if it admits a cyclic dualizing family.

Example 2.35
.

Suppose
{\cal V}
is a
*
-autonomous category with cyclic dualizing object
\bot
, one can show that
{\cal D}=\{\perp\colon\star\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.24657pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.24657pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.49313pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\star\}
is a cyclic dualizing family for its suspension
\cal B(V)
, since
A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\bot\cong A^{\perp}\cong\bot\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A
and
A\cong(A^{\perp})^{\perp}
, for all objects
A\in\mbox{${\cal V}$}
. So
\cal B(V)
is a cyclic
*
-autonomous bicategory.

In particular, consider
\mbox{${\cal V}$}={\rm Sup}
, the category of suplattices and suplattice homomorphisms (functions that preserve all joins).
Sup
is a
*
-autonomous category with cyclic dualizing object
\Omega^{op}
, with
A\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}\Omega^{op}\cong\Omega^{op}\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,A\cong A^{\circ}
, where
A^{\circ}
denotes the opposite poset of
A
, and
A\cong(A^{\circ})^{\circ}
, for all
A
. Therefore,
{\cal B}({\rm Sup})
is a cyclic
*
-autonomous bicategory with cyclic dualizing family
\mbox{${\cal D}$\,\,}=\{\Omega^{op}\colon\star\rightarrow\star\}
.

It will come as no surprise then that every cyclic
*
-autonomous bicategory is a linear bicategory by the de Morgan equations. It is remarked in
[
9
]
without a detailed proof as it would largely follow the same computation described in
[
11
]
, in the case of LDCs and
*
-autonomous categories. We describe below some of the key details.

Lemma 2.36
.

If
{\cal B}
is a cyclic
*
-autonomous bicategory, then there is an invertible 2-cell

A^{\perp}\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,B\xrightarrow{\sim}(B^{\perp}\otimes A^{\perp})^{\perp}\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad B\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}C^{\perp}\xrightarrow{\sim}(C^{\perp}\otimes B^{\perp})^{\perp}

for all
A\colon X\rightarrow Y
,
B\colon Y\rightarrow Z
and
C\colon Z\rightarrow W
.

Proof.

Suppose
{\cal D}=\{{\bot_{X}}\colon X\rightarrow X\}
is a cyclic dualizing family for
{\cal B}
. Then composition with
\delta_{B,Z}
induces the transpose invertible 2-cell

A^{\perp}\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,B\xrightarrow{\sim}A^{\perp}\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,((\bot_{Z}\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}B)\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,\bot_{Z})

Since
\bot_{Z}\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}B\cong B^{\perp}
and by Proposition
2.14
, it follows that

A^{\perp}\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,((\bot_{Z}\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}B)\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,\bot_{Z})\xrightarrow{\sim}(B^{\perp}\otimes A^{\perp})\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,\bot_{Z}=(B^{\perp}\otimes A^{\perp})^{\perp}

and the desired 2-cell follows. Similarly for the second result.
∎

Theorem 2.37
.

Every cyclic
*
-autonomous bicategory is a linear bicategory.

Proof.

Suppose
{\cal D}=\{{\bot_{X}}\colon X\rightarrow X\}
is a cyclic dualizing family for
{\cal B}
. Given
A\colon X\rightarrow Y
and
B\colon Y\rightarrow Z
, define
A\oplus B=(B^{\perp}\otimes A^{\perp})^{\perp}
.

Then
\oplus
is associative and has identity 1-cells
\bot_{X}=\top_{X}^{\perp}\colon X\rightarrow X
:

\displaystyle A\oplus(B\oplus C)

\displaystyle=A\oplus(C^{\perp}\otimes B^{\perp})^{\perp}\cong((C^{\perp}\otimes B^{\perp})\otimes A^{\perp})^{\perp}

\displaystyle\cong(C^{\perp}\otimes(B^{\perp}\otimes A^{\perp}))^{\perp}\cong(C^{\perp}\otimes(A\oplus B)^{\perp})^{\perp}

\displaystyle=(A\oplus B)\oplus C

and
\top_{X}^{\perp}\oplus A\cong A\cong A\oplus\top_{Y}^{\perp}
.

To see that
{\cal B}
is a linear bicategory, we will define the left linear distribution

A\otimes(B\oplus C)\rightarrow(A\otimes B)\oplus C

Since
A\otimes(B\oplus C)\cong A\otimes(B^{\perp}\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,C)
and
(A\otimes B)\oplus C\cong(A\otimes B)^{\perp}\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,C\cong(B^{\perp}\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A)\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,C
, by Lemma
2.36
; it suffices to define a 2-cell

A\otimes(B^{\perp}\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,C)\rightarrow(B^{\perp}\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A)\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,C

or equivalently, its transpose

(B^{\perp}\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A)\otimes(A\otimes(B^{\perp}\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,C))\rightarrow C

For this, we can use the associator and the evaluation maps

(B^{\perp}\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A)\otimes(A\otimes(B^{\perp}\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,C))\rightarrow((B^{\perp}\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A)\otimes A)\otimes(B^{\perp}\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,C)\rightarrow B^{\perp}\otimes(B^{\perp}\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,C)\rightarrow C

Similarly, we get a 2-cell
(A\oplus B)\otimes C\rightarrow A\oplus(B\otimes C)
, as desired.
∎

The last section of this article will be a discussion of several examples of non-posetal cyclic
*
-autonomous bicategories, including Quant and Qtld.

3
LD
-quantales and linear quantaloids

It is immediate that Girard quantaloids are locally posetal cyclic
*
-autonomous bicategories. In particular, the second composition is defined as the dual of the first. An alternate approach to the model theory of linear logic is that of linear bicategories where the tensor and par are taken as primitive. We define what the analogous structure would be for quantaloids below.

Definition 3.1
.

A linear quantaloid is a locally small category
{\cal Q}
whose hom-sets are complete lattices with binary operations
\otimes
and
\oplus
, and families of distinguished morphisms
\{\top_{a}~|~a\in{\rm ob}\mbox{${\cal Q}$}\}
and
\{\bot_{a}~|~a\in{\rm ob}\mbox{${\cal Q}$}\}
such that

•

(\mbox{${\cal Q}$},\otimes,\top_{a})
and
(\mbox{${\cal Q}$}^{co},\oplus,\bot_{a})
are quantaloids,

•

for all
f\colon a\rightarrow b,g\colon b\rightarrow c,h\colon c\rightarrow d\in\mbox{${\cal Q}$}
,

f\otimes(g\oplus h)\leq(f\otimes g)\oplus h\quad{\rm and}\quad(f\oplus g)\otimes h\leq f\oplus(g\otimes h)

Every Girard quantaloid is a linear quantaloid and we have the following obvious observation.

Lemma 3.2
.

A linear quantaloid is a linear bicategory.

Definition 3.3
.

If
{\cal Q}
and
{\cal Q}
’ are linear quantaloid, a linear quantaloid homomoprhism
F=(F_{\otimes},F_{\oplus})\colon\mbox{${\cal Q}$}\rightarrow\mbox{${\cal Q}$}^{\prime}
is a linear functor such that
F_{\otimes}
and
F_{\oplus}
are quantaloid homomorphisms.

A main result of the present work will be new examples of linear bicategories which are linear quantaloids. To construct these, we need the definition of the analogous linear structure for quantales. These are the
LD
-quantales as defined below.

Definition 3.4
.

An
LD
-quantale is a complete lattice
Q
with operations
\otimes
and
\oplus
and elements
\top

and
\perp
such that

•

(Q,\otimes,\top)
and
(Q^{op},\oplus,\bot)
are quantales.

•

for all
a,b,c\in Q
,

a\otimes(b\oplus c)\leq(a\otimes b)\oplus c\quad{\rm and}\quad(a\oplus b)\otimes c\leq a\oplus(b\otimes c)

Clearly a Girard quantale is a LD-quantale.

Example 3.5
.

(i)

\mbox{$\sf Rel$}(X)
, the poset of relations on a set
X
, is a Girard quantale with cyclic dualizing element
\Delta_{X}^{c}
(see Proposition 1.2 in
[
26
]
), and therefore is a LD-quantale.

(ii)

Every locale is a distributive lattice and a quantale, therefore it is a LD-quantale with
\otimes=\wedge
and
\oplus=\vee
. Rosenthal remarks that a locale is a Girard quantale if and only if it is a complete boolean algebra with
0
its dualizing element
[
26
]
.

•

Two-chain
\Omega
is a Girard quantale, and therefore a LD-quantale.

•

Three-chain
3
is the smallest Heyting algebra, which is not Boolean as the law of excluded middle doesn’t hold:

1/2\vee(0\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}1/2)=1/2\vee 0=1/2\neq 1

As such it is not a Girard quantale, but it is nonetheless a LD-quantale.

•

{\sf P_{max}}
is a LD-quantale with
\oplus={\rm min}
and
\bot=\infty
, but it is not a Girard quantale as
\infty
is not a dualizing element:

{\rm if}\quad a\neq\infty,\quad a\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,\infty=\infty\implies(a\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,\infty)\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,\infty=0

•

{\cal O}(X)
is Girard if and only if
\forall
open
U
, the interior of the closure of
U
is itself, i.e.,
U
is a regular open set. Many topological spaces do not satisfy this requirement, e.g.
{\cal O}(\mathbb{R})
with the standard Euclidean topology on
\mathbb{R}
is not Girard, but is nonetheless a LD-quantale.

(iii)

Consider the unit interval with multiplication
([0,1],\cdot,1)
. It becomes a LD-quantale
([0,1],\cdot,\oplus)
when considering truncated addition
a\oplus b={\sf min}(a+b,1)
for its par structure with unit
\bot=0
.
([0,1]^{op},\oplus,0)
is a quantale since

\bigwedge_{\alpha}{\sf min}(a+b_{\alpha},1)={\sf min}(a+\bigwedge_{\alpha}b_{\alpha},1)

Linear distributivities hold as

a\cdot(b+c)=(a\cdot b)+(a\cdot c)\leq(a\cdot b)+c\quad\quad\forall a\in[0,1]

It is not however a Girard quantale as
0
is not a dualizing element:

{\rm if}\quad a\neq 0,\quad a\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,0=0\implies(a\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,0)\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,0=1

Since
([0,1],\cdot,1)\,\cong\,{\sf P_{+}}
, we can use the isomorphims to define a par structure on
{\sf P_{+}}
:
a\oplus b={\sf max}(-{\sf ln}({\sf exp}(-a)+{\sf exp}(-b)),0)
and
\bot=\infty
. Then,
{\sf P_{+}}=([0,\infty]^{op},+,\oplus)
is a LD-quantale isomorphic to
([0,1],\cdot,\oplus)
.

Remark 3.6
.

Given a locale
L
, its booleanization
{\sf Bool}(L)=\{a\in L\,|\,(a\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,\mbox{\bf{0}})\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,\mbox{\bf{0}}=a\}
is a complete Boolean algebra
[
3
]
. Therefore, viewing any locale as a LD-quantale, there is always a sub-locale
{\sf Bool}(L)
which is a Girard quantale.

Here is one more example of a LD-quantale which requires a little more discussion.

3.1
Shift monoids

In
[
11
]
, the authors introduce the notion of a
shift monoid
. These provide examples
of discrete linearly distributive categories which are not
*
-autonomous.

Definition 3.7
.

•

A shift monoid consists of a 4-tuple
\mbox{${\cal M}$}=(M,+,\top,a)
where
(M,+,\top)
is a commutative monoid and
a
is an
invertible element of
M
.

•

If
{\cal M}
is a shift monoid, define a second multiplication by

x\cdot y=x+y-a

Then this is a second monoid structure on
M
with unit given by
a
.

It is trivial to see that
x\cdot(y+z)=(x\cdot y)+z
and so every shift monoid is a discrete linearly distributive category
(M,+,\cdot)
.

We modify the notion of shift monoid as follows in order to construct
LD
-quantales. Recall that a commutative monoid
M
is

cancellative
if for all
a,b,c\in\ M
, one has

a+b=a+c\Rightarrow b=c

Proposition 3.8
.

•

Let
(M,+,\top)
be a cancellative commutative monoid.
We view
M
as a discrete poset and then add top and bottom
elements which we denote
1
and
0
. Denote this set as
M^{+}
, We then extend the addition on
M
:

\mbox{\bf{1}}+b=\mbox{\bf{1}}\mbox{ if $b\in M$ or $b=\mbox{\bf{1}}$}\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\mbox{\bf{0}}+b=\mbox{\bf{0}}\mbox{ for all $b\in M^{+}$}

Then
(M^{+},+,\top)
is a commutative quantale.

•

Let
\mbox{${\cal M}$}=(M,+,\top,a)
be furthermore a cancellative commutative shift monoid, then extend the second operation in the dual way:

\mbox{\bf{0}}\cdot b=\mbox{\bf{0}}\mbox{ if $b\in M$ or $b=\mbox{\bf{0}}$}\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\mbox{\bf{1}}\cdot b=\mbox{\bf{1}}\mbox{ for all $b\in M^{+}$}

Then
(M^{+},+,\cdot)
is a
LD
-quantale.

Remark 3.9
.

The cancellative property is needed to ensure that
+
preserves all supremums in
M^{+}
.

4
Q
-
Rel
as a linear bicategory

We now demonstrate that if
Q
is a Girard quantale, or more generally a
LD
-quantale,
Q
-
Rel
determines a linear quantaloid, providing new examples of linear bicategories.

Proposition 4.1
.

Q
-
Rel
is a Girard quantaloid with cyclic dualizing family
\mbox{${\cal D}$\,\,}=\{\bot_{X}\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}X\}
if and only if
Q
is a Girard quantale with cyclic dualizing element
\bot
, where
\bot=\bot_{1}(*,*)
,
\bot_{1}\colon 1\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}1
being the constant map between singleton sets
1=\{*\}
, and

\bot_{X}(x,x^{\prime})=\begin{cases}\bot&x=x^{\prime}\\
\mbox{\bf{1}}&x\neq x^{\prime}\end{cases}

(*)

Proof.

Suppose
Q
-
Rel
is a Girard quantaloid. Given
a\in Q
, let
R_{a}\colon 1\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}1
denote the
a
-valued constant relation. Since
R_{a}\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\bot_{1}=\bot_{1}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}R_{a}
and
R_{a}^{\perp\perp}=R_{a}
, it follows that
\bot=\bot_{1}(*,*)
is a cyclic dualizing element for
Q
, and so
Q
is a Girard quantale.

Consider a set
X
. Let
x^{\prime}\in X
and define
Q
-relation
R\colon 1\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}X
by

R(*,x)=\begin{cases}\top&x=x^{\prime}\cr\mbox{\bf{1}}&x\neq x^{\prime}\end{cases}

Now
(\bot_{X}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}R)(x,*)=\displaystyle\bigwedge_{\bar{x}}\bot_{X}(x,\bar{x})\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}R(*,{\bar{x}})=\bot_{X}(x,x^{\prime})

and

(R\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\bot_{1})(x,*)=R(*,x)\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\bot=\begin{cases}\bot&x=x^{\prime}\\
\mbox{\bf{1}}&x\neq x^{\prime}\end{cases}

since
\top\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\bot=\bot
. As
{\cal D}

is cyclic,
\bot_{X}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}r=r\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\bot_{1}
and consequently, (*) holds.

Suppose
Q
is a Girard quantale with cyclic dualizing element
\bot
. Define a family of
Q
-relations
{\cal D}

by
(*)
. Consider
R\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y
, then

(R\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\bot_{X})(y,x)=\displaystyle\bigwedge_{\bar{x}}R(\bar{x},y)\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\bot_{X}(\bar{x},x)=R(x,y)\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\bot=R(x,y)^{\perp}

(\bot_{Y}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}r)(y,x)=\displaystyle\bigwedge_{\bar{y}}d_{Y}(y,\bar{y})\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!-r(x,\bar{y})=\bot\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}R(x,y)=R(x,y)^{\perp}

and so
{\cal D}

is a dualizing family for
Q
-
Rel
as
R\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\bot_{X}=\bot_{Y}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}R
.
{\cal D}

being cyclic follows immediately and as such
Q
-
Rel
is a Girard quantaloid.
∎

Consequently, if
Q
is a Girard quantale, there is a second categorical structure on
Q
-
Rel
determining a linear bicategory. Indeed its second composition is obtained as the deMorgan dual of its standard composition

R\oplus S(x,z)=(\bigvee_{y\in Y}S(y,z)^{\perp}\otimes R(x,y)^{\perp})^{\perp}=\bigwedge_{y\in Y}(S(y,z)^{\perp}\otimes R(x,y)^{\perp})^{\perp}=\bigwedge_{y\in Y}R(x,y)\oplus S(y,z)

with identities
\bot_{X}
.

This generalizes further. Suppose
(Q,\otimes,\top)
and
(Q^{op},\oplus,\bot)
are quantales. Two notions of composition

\otimes,\oplus\colon\mbox{${\cal B}$}(X,Y)\times\mbox{${\cal B}$}(Y,Z)\longrightarrow\mbox{${\cal B}$}(X,Z)

are defined as follows: given
R\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y
and
S\colon Y\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Z
,

R\otimes S(x,z)=\bigvee_{y\in Y}(R(x,y)\otimes S(y,z))\mbox{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,}R\oplus S(x,z)=\bigwedge_{y\in Y}(R(x,y)\oplus S(y,z))

The identity 1-cells are given by:

\top_{X}(x,x^{\prime})=\begin{cases}\top&\mbox{if }x=x^{\prime}\\
\mbox{\bf{0}}&\mbox{if }x\neq x^{\prime}\end{cases}\mbox{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,}\bot_{X}(x,x^{\prime})=\begin{cases}\bot&\mbox{if }x=x^{\prime}\\
\mbox{\bf{1}}&\mbox{if }x\neq x^{\prime}\end{cases}

and we get:

Theorem 4.2
.

Q
is an
LD
-quantale if and only if
Q
-
Rel
is a linear quantaloid.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.9
,
(\mbox{$Q$-{\sf Rel}},\otimes,\top_{X})
and
(Q^{op}
-
{\sf Rel},\oplus,\bot_{X})\,\cong\,(\mbox{$Q$-{\sf Rel}}^{co},\oplus,\bot_{X})
are quantaloids as
(Q,\otimes,\top)
and
(Q^{op},\oplus,\bot)
are quantales, inheriting their structure from
Q
pointwise.

Suppose
(
Q
,\otimes,\oplus)
is an
LD
-quantale. Given

W\stackrel{{\scriptstyle R}}{{\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}}}X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle S}}{{\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}}}Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle T}}{{\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}}}Z

we have

R\otimes(S\oplus T)\leq(R\otimes S)\oplus T
if and only if, for all
w,z
,

\bigvee_{x}[R(w,x)\otimes(S\oplus T)(x,z)]\leq\bigwedge_{y}[(R\otimes S)(w,y)\oplus T(y,z)]

if and only if
R(w,x)\otimes(S\oplus T)(x,z)\leq(R\otimes S)(w,y)\oplus T(y,z)
, for all
w,x,y,z
. But,

\displaystyle R(w,x)\otimes(S\oplus T)(x,z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R(w,x)\otimes\bigwedge_{y}[S(x,y)\oplus T(y,z)]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle R(w,x)\otimes[S(x,y)\oplus T(y,z)]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle[R(w,x)\otimes S(x,y)]\oplus T(y,z)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigvee_{x}[R(w,x)\otimes S(x,y)]\oplus T(y,z)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(R\otimes S)(w,y)\oplus T(y,z)

The other inequality follows similarly and we conclude that
Q
-
Rel
is a linear quantaloid.

Conversely, suppose
Q
-
Rel
is a linear quantaloid. Then
a,b,c
in
Q
induces
1\!\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.96954pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.96954pt\raise 5.89168pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.89168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{R_{a}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.24657pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.49313pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.8pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}\!1\!\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.6168pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.6168pt\raise 5.92223pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.86113pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{R_{b}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.24657pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.49313pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.8pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}\!1\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.65704pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.65704pt\raise 5.89168pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.89168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{R_{c}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.24657pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.49313pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.8pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}\!1
in
Q
-
Rel
, and
R_{a}\otimes(R_{b}\oplus R_{c})\leq(R_{a}\otimes R_{b})\oplus R_{c}
implies
a\otimes(b\oplus c)\leq(a\otimes b)\oplus c
. Thus
Q
is an
LD
-quantale.
∎

Q
-
Rel
is a linear quantaloid for all locales
Q
as they are LD-quantales.

Example 4.3
.

(i)

2
-
Rel
\,\cong\,\mbox{$\sf Rel$}
is a Girard quantaloid, with its par composition given by deMorgan duality, the same as previously introduced in the preliminaries section.

(ii)

3
-
{\sf Rel}
is a linear quantaloid, which is not a Girard quantaloid, as
3
is the smallest locale that is not a complete Boolean algebra.

(iii)

{\sf P_{max}}
-
Rel
, the quantaloid of sets and “extended” distance relations, is a linear quantaloid, with its par composition defined, for
D_{1}\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y
and
D_{2}\colon Y\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}X
by

(D_{1}\oplus D_{2})(x,z)=\bigvee_{y\in Y}{\sf min}(D_{1}(x,y),D_{2}(y,z))

and par identities given by

\bot_{X}(x,x^{\prime})=\begin{cases}\infty&\mbox{if }x=x^{\prime}\\
0&\mbox{if }x\neq x^{\prime}\end{cases}

(iv)

[0,1]
-
Rel
is another linear quantaloid of sets and relations, with its par composition defined, for
D_{1}\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y
and
D_{2}\colon Y\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}X
by

(D_{1}\oplus D_{2})(x,z)=\bigwedge_{y\in Y}{\sf min}(D_{1}(x,y)+D_{2}(y,z),1)

and par identities given by

\bot_{X}(x,x^{\prime})=\begin{cases}0&\mbox{if }x=x^{\prime}\\
1&\mbox{if }x\neq x^{\prime}\end{cases}

Alternatively,
{\sf P_{+}}
-
Rel
with its par composition defined, for
D_{1}\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y
and
D_{2}\colon Y\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}X
by

(D_{1}\oplus D_{2})(x,z)=\bigvee_{y\in Y}{\sf max}(-{\sf ln}({\sf exp}(-D_{1}(x,y))+{\sf exp}(-D_{2}(y,z))),0)

and par identities given by

\bot_{X}(x,x^{\prime})=\begin{cases}\infty&\mbox{if }x=x^{\prime}\\
0&\mbox{if }x\neq x^{\prime}\end{cases}

5
Enriched in a linear quantaloid

Rosenthal demonstrated that the definition of a Girard quantaloid was closed under various constructions, in particular if
{\cal Q}
is a Girard quantaloid, then
{\cal Q}
-
Mod
is one as well
[
26
]
. This can be easily extended into a necessary and sufficient condition.

Proposition 5.1
.

{\cal Q}
-
Mod
is a Girard quantaloid with cyclic dualizing family
\{\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{M}\colon M\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}M\}
if and only if
{\cal Q}
is a Girard quantaloid with cyclic dualizing family
\{\bot_{a}\colon a\rightarrow a\}
, where
\bot_{a}=\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{M_{a}}(*,*)
,
M_{a}=(1,\rho_{a})
being the
{\cal Q}
-category defined by
\rho_{a}(*)=a
and
M_{a}(*,*)=\top_{a}
, and

\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{M}(x,x^{\prime})=M(x^{\prime},x)\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\bot_{\rho(x^{\prime})}

Proof.

Suppose
{\cal Q}
-
Mod
is a Girard quantaloid with cyclic dualizing family
\{\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{M}\colon M\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}M\}
. Given an object
a
in
{\cal Q}
, define the
{\cal Q}
-category
M_{a}=(1,\rho_{a})
as indicated above. Define a family of morphisms
\mbox{${\cal D}$\,\,}=\{\bot_{a}\colon a\rightarrow a\}
in
{\cal Q}
by
\bot_{a}=\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{M_{a}}(*,*)
.

Given a morphism
f\colon a\rightarrow b
in
{\cal Q}
, consider its image
\Theta_{f}\colon M_{a}\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}M_{b}
under the embedding of
{\cal Q}
into
{\cal Q}
-
Mod
. Then,

f\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\bot_{a}=\Theta_{f}(*,*)\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}~\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{M_{a}}(*,*)=\bigwedge_{x\in\{*\}}\Theta_{f}(x,*)\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}~\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{M_{a}}(x,*)=(\Theta_{f}\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}~\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{M_{a}})(*,*)=\Theta_{f}^{\perp}(*,*)

\bot_{b}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}f=\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{M_{b}}(*,*)~\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}\Theta_{f}(*,*)=\bigwedge_{x\in\{*\}}\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{M_{b}}(*,x)~\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}\Theta_{f}(*,x)=(\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{M_{b}}~\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}\Theta_{f})(*,*)=\Theta_{f}^{\perp}(*,*)

implying
{\cal D}

is a cyclic family of morphisms in
{\cal Q}
and
{\cal D}

being dualizing follows similarly.

The sufficieny proof is shown in Theorem 3.1 in
[
26
]
.
∎

Notice that if
{\cal Q}
is a Girard quantaloid, then for each
{\cal Q}
-category
M=(X,\rho)
and
(x,x^{\prime})\in X
, there is a morphism
M(x^{\prime},x)^{\perp}\colon\rho(x)\rightarrow\rho(x^{\prime})
satisfying
\forall x,x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\in X

M(x,x)^{\bot}\leq\bot_{\rho(x)}\qquad M(x,x^{\prime\prime})^{\bot}\leq M(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})^{\bot}\oplus M(x,x^{\prime})^{\bot}

Therefore the map
M^{\perp}\colon(x,x^{\prime})\mapsto M(x^{\prime},x)^{\perp}
, coupled with
(X,\rho)
is a
\mbox{${\cal Q}$}^{co}
-category, where
\mbox{${\cal Q}$}^{co}
is the quantaloid with the deMorgan dual composition
\oplus
and identities
\bot_{X}
.

M^{\perp}
is morevoer a
{\cal Q}
-module
M\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}M
:
\forall x,x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\in X

M(x^{\prime},x)^{\bot}\otimes M(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})\leq M(x^{\prime\prime},x)^{\bot}\qquad M(x,x^{\prime})\otimes M(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})^{\bot}\leq M(x^{\prime\prime},x)^{\bot}

While
M
becomes a
\mbox{${\cal Q}$}^{co}
-module
M^{\perp}\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}M^{\perp}
:
\forall x,x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\in X

M(x,x^{\prime\prime})\leq M(x^{\prime},x)^{\bot}\oplus M(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})\qquad M(x,x^{\prime\prime})\leq M(x,x^{\prime})\oplus M(x^{\prime\prime},x^{\prime})^{\bot}

All together, this entails that
(M,M^{\bot})\colon X\rightarrow\mbox{${\cal Q}$}
is a linear functor (as defined in
[
9
]
), when
X
is viewed as a degenerate indiscrete linear bicategory.

Each
{\cal Q}
-module
\Theta\colon M\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}N
interacts coherently with this structure and it determines a
\mbox{${\cal Q}$}^{co}
-module
M^{\perp}\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}N^{\perp}
:
\forall x,x^{\prime}\in X,y,y^{\prime}\in Y

\Theta(x,y^{\prime})\leq M(x,x^{\prime})^{\bot}\oplus\Theta(x,y)\qquad\Theta(x,y)\leq\Theta(x,y^{\prime})\oplus N(y,y^{\prime})^{\bot}

As with every Girard quantaloid, we can define a second composition on
{\cal Q}
-
Mod
. Given
M\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Theta}}{{\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}}}N\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Pi}}{{\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}}}P
,
