\Theta\otimes\Pi\colon M\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}P
and
\Theta\oplus\Pi\colon M\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}P
are defined by

(\Theta\otimes\Pi)(x,z)=\bigvee_{y\in Y}\Theta(x,y)\otimes\Pi(y,z)\quad{\rm and}\quad(\Theta\oplus\Pi)(x,z)=\bigwedge_{y\in Y}\Theta(x,y)\oplus\Pi(y,z)

The identities 1-cells for
\otimes
and
\oplus
are given by
\mbox{$\top\!\!\!\top$}_{M}
and
\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{M}^{\bot}
respectively, where

\mbox{$\top\!\!\!\top$}_{M}(x,x^{\prime})=M(x,x^{\prime})\quad{\rm and}\quad\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{M}(x,x^{\prime})=M(x^{\prime},x)^{\bot}

The above discussion motivates the following new definitions which generalize
{\cal Q}
-
Mod
to the case where
{\cal Q}
is a linear quantaloid as follows.

Let
(\mbox{${\cal Q}$},\otimes,\top_{a})
and
(\mbox{${\cal Q}$}^{co},\oplus,\bot_{a})
be quantaloids.

Definition 5.2
.

A linear
{\cal Q}
-category is a pair
M=(X,\rho)
where:

•

X is a set.

•

\rho
is a function
X\rightarrow ob\mbox{${\cal Q}$}
,

•

there is a function, called the
\otimes
-enrichment, assigning a morphism
M_{\otimes}(x,x^{\prime})\colon\rho(x)\rightarrow\rho(x^{\prime})
in
{\cal Q}
to each pair
(x,x^{\prime})\in X\times X
such that
\forall x,x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\in X

\top_{\rho(x)}\leq M_{\otimes}(x,x)\qquad M_{\otimes}(x,x^{\prime})\otimes M_{\otimes}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})\leq M_{\otimes}(x,x^{\prime\prime})

•

there is a function, called the
\oplus
-enrichment, assigning a morphism
M_{\oplus}(x,x^{\prime})\colon\rho(x)\rightarrow\rho(x^{\prime})
in
{\cal Q}
to each pair
(x,x^{\prime})\in X\times X
such that
\forall x,x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\in X

M_{\oplus}(x,x)\leq\bot_{\rho(x)}\qquad M_{\oplus}(x,x^{\prime\prime})\leq M_{\oplus}(x,x^{\prime})\oplus M_{\oplus}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})

satisfying the following inequalities
\forall x,x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\in X
,

M_{\otimes}(x,x^{\prime\prime})\leq M_{\oplus}(x,x^{\prime})\oplus M_{\otimes}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})\qquad M_{\otimes}(x,x^{\prime\prime})\leq M_{\otimes}(x,x^{\prime})\oplus M_{\oplus}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})

M_{\otimes}(x,x^{\prime})\otimes M_{\oplus}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})\leq M_{\oplus}(x,x^{\prime\prime})\qquad M_{\oplus}(x,x^{\prime})\otimes M_{\otimes}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})\leq M_{\oplus}(x,x^{\prime\prime})

More succinctly, if
{\cal Q}
is a linear quantaloid, a linear
{\cal Q}
-category is a linear functor from
X
to
{\cal Q}
, where
X
is viewed as a degenerate indiscrete linear bicategory.

Definition 5.3
.

Consider linear
{\cal Q}
-categories
M=(X,\rho_{M})
and
N=(Y,\rho_{N})
. A linear
{\cal Q}
-module
\Theta\colon M\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}N
consists of an assignment of a morphism
\Theta(x,y)\colon\rho_{M}(x)\rightarrow\rho_{N}(y)
to every pair
(x,y)\in X\times Y
such that
\forall x,x^{\prime}\in X,y,y^{\prime}\in Y,

\Theta(x,y)\otimes N_{\otimes}(y,y^{\prime})\leq\Theta(x,y^{\prime})\qquad M_{\otimes}(x,x^{\prime})\otimes\Theta(x^{\prime},y)\leq\Theta(x,y)

\Theta(x,y)\leq\Theta(x,y^{\prime})\oplus N_{\oplus}(y^{\prime},y)\qquad\Theta(x,y)\leq M_{\oplus}(x,x^{\prime})\oplus\Theta(x^{\prime},y)

Remark 5.4
.

•

Given a linear
{\cal Q}
-category
M=(X,\rho)
,
M_{\otimes}=(X,\rho)
is a
{\cal Q}
-category and
M_{\oplus}=(X,\rho)
is a
\mbox{${\cal Q}$}^{co}
-category. Further, given a linear
{\cal Q}
-module
\Theta\colon M\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}N
,
\Theta
is a
{\cal Q}
-module
M_{\otimes}\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}N_{\otimes}
and a
\mbox{${\cal Q}$}^{co}
-module
M_{\oplus}\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}N_{\oplus}
.

•

It was shown by Cockett, Koslowski and Seely that representable poly-bicategories are linear bicategories and poly-functors between them are linear functors
[
10
]
. When viewing linear
{\cal Q}
-categories as a poly-functors between representable poly-bicategories, a linear
{\cal Q}
-module is a poly-module between the poly-functors (as defined in Def 4.1
[
10
]
).

We can now define bicategory structures attached to the above data.

Definition 5.5
.

Define
{\cal Q}
-
Mod
to consist of the following data:

•

0-cells are linear
{\cal Q}
-categories

•

given a pair of
{\cal Q}
-categories
M
and
N
, there is a category with 1-cells the linear
{\cal Q}
-modules
M\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}N
and 2-cells the pointwise inequalities, i.e.,

\Theta\leq\Pi\Leftrightarrow~\forall(x,y)\in X\times Y,\quad\Theta(x,y)\leq\Pi(x,y)

•

given
{\cal Q}
-categories
M
,
N
and
P
, there are two composition functors
\otimes,\oplus
defined for
\Theta\colon M\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}N
and
\Pi\colon N\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}P
by

(\Theta\otimes\Pi)(x,z)=\bigvee_{y\in Y}\Theta(x,y)\otimes\Pi(y,z)\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad(\Theta\oplus\Pi)(x,z)=\bigwedge_{y\in Y}\Theta(x,y)\oplus\Pi(y,z)

•

given every
{\cal Q}
-category
M
, there are identity 1-cells
\mbox{$\top\!\!\!\top$}_{M},\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{M}\colon M\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}M
defined by

\mbox{$\top\!\!\!\top$}_{M}(x,x^{\prime})=M_{\otimes}(x,x^{\prime})\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{M}(x,x^{\prime})=M_{\oplus}(x,x^{\prime})

And we can show:

Theorem 5.6
.

(\mbox{\mbox{${\cal Q}$}-{\sf Mod}},\otimes,\oplus)
is a linear quantaloid if and only if
(\mbox{${\cal Q}$},\otimes,\oplus)
is a linear quantaloid.

This will be an immediate consequences of similar results for linear
{\cal Q}
-matrices and linear monads in
{\cal Q}
, so we will delay proving the above theorem.

Another construction that was shown to be Girard by Rosenthal in
[
26
]
is the quantaloid of
{\cal Q}
-matrices:

Proposition 5.7
.

Matr
{\cal Q}
is a Girard quantaloid with cyclic dualizing family
\{\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{(X,\gamma)}\colon(X,\gamma)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(X,\gamma)\}
if and only if
{\cal Q}
is a Girard quantaloid with cyclic dualizing family
\{\bot_{a}\colon a\rightarrow a\}
, where
\bot_{a}={\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{(1,\gamma_{a})}}_{*,*}
,
(1,\gamma_{a})
consisting of the singleton set
1
and the function
\gamma_{a}\colon*\mapsto a
, and

{\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{(X,\gamma)}}_{x,x^{\prime}}=\begin{cases}\bot_{\gamma(x)}&x=x^{\prime}\\
\mbox{\bf{1}}_{\gamma(x),\gamma(x^{\prime})}&x\neq x^{\prime}\end{cases}

Proof.

The forward direction follows similarly to the proof in the case of
{\cal Q}
-
Mod
, while the backwards direction proved by Theorem 3.2 in
[
26
]
.
∎

If
(\mbox{${\cal Q}$},\otimes,\top_{a})
and
(\mbox{${\cal Q}$}^{co},\oplus,\bot_{a})
are quantaloids, then
Matr
{\cal Q}
inherits a second bicategorical structure. The two notions of composition are defined as follows: given
{\cal Q}
-matrices
(X,\gamma)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}}}(Y,\phi)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s}}{{\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}}}(Z,\chi)
,
r\otimes s,r\oplus s\colon(X,\gamma)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(Z,\chi)
are defined by

(r\otimes s)_{x,z}=\bigvee_{y\in Y}r_{x,y}\otimes s_{y,z}\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad(r\oplus s)_{x,z}=\bigwedge_{y\in Y}r_{x,y}\oplus s_{y,z}

Identity 1-cells
\mbox{$\top\!\!\!\top$}_{(X,\gamma)},\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{(X,\gamma)}\colon(X,\gamma)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(X,\gamma)
are defined by

{\mbox{$\top\!\!\!\top$}_{(X,\gamma)}}_{x,x^{\prime}}=\begin{cases}\top_{\gamma(x)}&\mbox{if }x=x^{\prime}\\
\mbox{\bf{0}}_{\gamma(x),\phi(x^{\prime})}&\mbox{if }x\neq x^{\prime}\end{cases}\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad{\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{(X,\gamma)}}_{x,x^{\prime}}=\begin{cases}\bot_{\gamma(x)}&\mbox{if }x=x^{\prime}\\
\mbox{\bf{1}}_{\gamma(x),\phi(x^{\prime})}&\mbox{if }x\neq x^{\prime}\end{cases}

Theorem 5.8
.

Matr
{\cal Q}
is a linear quantaloid if and only if
{\cal Q}
is a linear quantaloid.

Proof.

The proof is identical to that of Theorem
4.2
, replacing objects in a quantale
Q
by morphisms in
{\cal Q}
.
∎

Remark 5.9
.

As in the case of ordinary quantales, it is immediate that for a LD-quantale
Q
, the linear quantaloid
{\sf Matr}{\cal B}(Q)
is isomorphic to
Q
-
Rel
.

Finally, as one might expect, taking monads and modules in a Girard quantaloid remains Girard.

Proposition 5.10
.

Mon
{\cal Q}
is a Girard quantaloid with cyclic dualizing family
\{\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{(a,m)}\colon(a,m)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(a,m)\}
if and only if
{\cal Q}
is a Girard quantaloid with cyclic dualizing family
\{\bot_{a}\colon a\rightarrow a\}
, where
\bot_{a}=\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{(a,\top_{a})}
,
(a,\top_{a})
being the trivial monad on
a
, and

\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{(a,m)}=m\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\bot_{a}

Proof.

The forward direction is immediate from the trivial monad embedding of
{\cal Q}
into
Mon
{\cal Q}
, while the backwards direction is proven by Theorem 3.3 in
[
26
]
.
∎

As in the case of
{\cal Q}
-
Mod
, this can be generalized to the linear setting.

Let
(\mbox{${\cal Q}$},\otimes,\top_{a})
and
(\mbox{${\cal Q}$}^{co},\oplus,\bot_{a})
be quantaloids.

Definition 5.11

(Def 4.13
[
9
]
)
.

A linear monad
(a,m)=(a,m_{\otimes},m_{\oplus})
in
{\cal Q}
is a pair of compatible
\otimes
-monad
(a,m_{\otimes})
and
\oplus
-comonad
(a,m_{\oplus})
, i.e., it consists of

•

an object
a

•

a morphism
m_{\otimes}:a\rightarrow a
such that

\top_{a}\leq m_{\otimes}\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad m_{\otimes}\otimes m_{\otimes}\leq m_{\otimes}

•

a morphism
m_{\oplus}:a\rightarrow a
such that

m_{\oplus}\leq\bot_{a}\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad m_{\oplus}\leq m_{\oplus}\oplus m_{\oplus}

satisfying the following inequalities:

m_{\otimes}\leq m_{\oplus}\oplus m_{\otimes}\qquad m_{\otimes}\leq m_{\otimes}\oplus m_{\oplus}

m_{\otimes}\otimes m_{\oplus}\leq m_{\oplus}\qquad m_{\oplus}\otimes m_{\otimes}\leq m_{\oplus}

Definition 5.12
.

Let
(a,m)
and
(b,n)
be linear monads in
{\cal Q}
. A linear
(m,n)
-module (or monad module)
f\colon(a,m)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(b,n)
is a morphism
f\colon a\rightarrow b
in
{\cal Q}
which is
\otimes
-monad module
f\colon(a,m_{\otimes})\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(b,n_{\otimes})
and a
\oplus
-comonad module
f\colon(a,m_{\oplus})\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(b,n_{\oplus})
, i.e., satisfies the following inequalities:

f\otimes n_{\otimes}\leq f\qquad m_{\otimes}\otimes f\leq f

f\leq m_{\oplus}\oplus f\qquad f\leq f\oplus n_{\oplus}

Definition 5.13
.

Define
Mon
{\cal Q}
to consist of the following data:

•

0-cells are linear monads in
{\cal Q}

•

given a pair of linear monads
(a,m)
and
(b,n)
, there is a category with 1-cells the linear
(m,n)
-modules and 2-cells are inherited from
{\cal Q}

•

given linear monads
(a,m)
,
(b,n)
and
(c,p)
, there are two composition functors
\otimes,\oplus
which are inherited from
{\cal Q}

•

given every linear monad
(a,m)
, there are identity 1-cells
\mbox{$\top\!\!\!\top$}_{(a,m)},\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{(a,m)}\colon(a,m)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(a,m)
defined by

\mbox{$\top\!\!\!\top$}_{(a,m)}=m_{\otimes}\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{(a,m)}=m_{\oplus}

Lemma 5.14
.

If
{\cal Q}
is a linear quantaloid, then
(\mbox{{\sf Mon}\mbox{${\cal Q}$}},\otimes,\mbox{$\top\!\!\!\top$}_{(a,m)})
and
(\mbox{{\sf Mon}\mbox{${\cal Q}$}}^{co},\oplus,\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{(a,m)})
are quantaloids.

Proof.

(\mbox{{\sf Mon}\mbox{${\cal Q}$}},\otimes,\mbox{$\top\!\!\!\top$}_{(a,m)})
is a category with a well-defined composition
\otimes
: given linear monad modules
f\colon(a,m)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(b,n)
and
g\colon(b,n)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(c,p)
, it is immediate that
f\otimes g\colon a\rightarrow c
is a
\otimes
-monad module since
f,g
are
\otimes
-monad modules and, by linear distributivity,
f\otimes g\colon a\rightarrow c
is a
\oplus
-comonad module as follows.

f\otimes g\leq(m_{\oplus}\oplus f)\otimes g\leq m_{\oplus}\oplus(f\otimes g)\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad f\otimes g\leq f\otimes(g\oplus p_{\oplus})\leq(f\otimes g)\oplus p_{\oplus}

as
f,g
are
\oplus
-comonad modules.
Identities
\mbox{$\top\!\!\!\top$}_{(a,m)}\colon(a,m)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(a,m)
are also well-defined as
m_{\otimes}
is a linear monad module:

m_{\otimes}\otimes m_{\otimes}\leq m_{\otimes}\quad,\quad\quad m_{\otimes}\leq m_{\oplus}\oplus m_{\otimes}\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad m_{\otimes}\leq m_{\otimes}\oplus m_{\oplus}

by the definition of linear
{\cal Q}
-categories. The quantaloid structure is directly inherited from
(\mbox{${\cal Q}$},\otimes,\top)
. Similarly,
(\mbox{{\sf Mon}\mbox{${\cal Q}$}}^{co},\oplus,\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{(a,m)})
is a quantaloid.
∎

Then we get this result:

Theorem 5.15
.

Mon
{\cal Q}
is a linear quantaloid if and only if
{\cal Q}
is a linear quantaloid.

Proof.

Suppose
{\cal Q}
is a linear quantaloid, then it is immediate that
Mon
{\cal Q}
is a linear quantaloid as compositions
\otimes
and
\oplus
are inherited directly.

Suppose
Mon
{\cal Q}
is a linear quantaloid, consider the mapping of each object
a\in\mbox{${\cal Q}$}
to the trivial linear monad
(a,\top_{a},\bot_{a})
and each morphism
f\colon a\rightarrow b
to itself viewed as the trivial linear monad module
f\colon(a,\top_{a},\bot_{a})\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(b,\top_{b},\bot_{b})
. Then,
\forall f\colon a\rightarrow b,g\colon b\rightarrow c,h\colon c\rightarrow d
, we know
f\otimes(g\oplus h)\leq(f\otimes g)\oplus h
and
(f\oplus g)\otimes h\leq f\oplus(g\otimes h)
, since the inequalities hold when they are viewed as 1-cells in
Mon
{\cal Q}
.
∎

We now return to the proof of Theorem
5.6
:

Proof.

Suppose
{\cal Q}
is a linear quantaloid, then
Matr
{\cal Q}
is a linear quantaloid and therefore we can perform the linear monad construction and see that
{\sf Mon}\mbox{{\sf Matr}\mbox{${\cal Q}$}}
is a linear quantaloid. By looking at the definitions, it is immediate that
({\sf Mon}\mbox{{\sf Matr}\mbox{${\cal Q}$}},\otimes,\mbox{$\top\!\!\!\top$}_{(X,\gamma),m)})
is biequivalent to
(\mbox{\mbox{${\cal Q}$}-{\sf Mod}},\otimes,\mbox{$\top\!\!\!\top$}_{M})
and
({\sf Mon}\mbox{{\sf Matr}\mbox{${\cal Q}$}}^{co},\oplus,\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{(X,\gamma),m)})
is biequivalent to
(\mbox{\mbox{${\cal Q}$}-{\sf Mod}}^{co},\oplus,\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{M})
. Thus
{\cal Q}
-
Mod
and
\mbox{\mbox{${\cal Q}$}-{\sf Mod}}^{co}
are quantaloids.

Moreover, given linear
{\cal Q}
-modules
\Theta\colon M\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}N
,
\Pi\colon N\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}P
and
\Sigma\colon P\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}R
,

\Theta\otimes(\Pi\oplus\Sigma)\leq(\Theta\otimes\Pi)\oplus\Sigma\quad{\rm and}\quad(\Theta\oplus\Pi)\otimes\Sigma\leq\Theta\oplus(\Pi\otimes\Sigma)

since the inequalities hold when they are viewed as linear
(M,N),(N,P)
and
(P,R)
-monad modules in
{\sf Mon}\mbox{{\sf Matr}\mbox{${\cal Q}$}}
.

Suppose
{\cal Q}
-
Mod
is a linear quantaloid, then consider the mapping of each object
a\in\mbox{${\cal Q}$}
to the trivial linear
{\cal Q}
-category
M_{a}=(1,\rho_{a})
where
1=\{*\}
is the singleton set,
\rho_{a}(*)=a
,
{M_{a}}_{\otimes}=\top_{a}
and
{M_{a}}_{\oplus}=\bot_{a}
, each morphism to
f\colon a\rightarrow b
to the trivial linear
{\cal Q}
-module
\Theta_{f}\colon M_{a}\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}M_{b}
, where
\Theta_{f}(*,*)=f
. This mapping ensures the linear distributivities hold in
{\cal Q}
as they hold
{\cal Q}
-
Mod
.
∎

Remark 5.16
.

As expected, the constructions are related: let
{\cal Q}
be a linear quantaloid, then
\mbox{\mbox{${\cal Q}$}-{\sf Mod}}\cong{\sf Mon}\mbox{{\sf Matr}\mbox{${\cal Q}$}}
. Therefore, given any LD-quantale
Q
, we can then consider linear quantaloid
{\sf Mon}(\mbox{$Q$-{\sf Rel}})
which is isomorphic to
{\sf Mon}{\sf Matr}{\cal B}(Q)
and
{\cal B}(Q)
-
{\sf Mod}
.

Example 5.17
.

(i)

Let
\sf Rel
be the Girard quantaloid of sets and relations, then consider the Girard quantaloid
{\sf Mon}(\mbox{$\sf Rel$})
. Its objects are sets
X
endowed with relations
\leq_{\otimes}
satisfying

x\leq_{\otimes}x\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad(x\leq_{\otimes}x^{\prime}\wedge x^{\prime}\leq_{\otimes}x^{\prime\prime})\implies x\leq_{\otimes}x^{\prime\prime}

and
\leq_{\oplus}
satisfying

x\leq_{\oplus}x^{\prime}\implies x\neq x^{\prime}\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad x\leq_{\oplus}x^{\prime\prime}\implies(\forall x^{\prime},x\leq_{\oplus}x^{\prime}\vee x^{\prime}\leq_{\oplus}x^{\prime\prime})

such that

x\leq_{\otimes}x^{\prime\prime}\implies(\forall x^{\prime},x\leq_{\oplus}x^{\prime}\vee x^{\prime}\leq_{\otimes}x^{\prime\prime})\quad\quad{\rm,}\quad\quad x\leq_{\otimes}x^{\prime\prime}\implies(\forall x^{\prime},x\leq_{\otimes}x^{\prime}\vee x^{\prime}\leq_{\oplus}x^{\prime\prime})

(x\leq_{\otimes}x^{\prime}\wedge x^{\prime}\leq_{\oplus}x^{\prime\prime})\implies x\leq_{\oplus}x^{\prime\prime}\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad(x\leq_{\oplus}x^{\prime}\wedge x^{\prime}\leq_{\otimes}x^{\prime\prime})\implies x\leq_{\oplus}x^{\prime\prime}

These are preordered sets
(X,\leq_{\otimes})
with an additional relation
\leq_{\oplus}
which interacts coherently with the preorder. Its arrows are relations
R\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y
satisfying

(xRy^{\prime}\wedge y^{\prime}\leq^{Y}_{\otimes}y)\implies xRy\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad(x\leq_{\otimes}^{X}x^{\prime}\wedge x^{\prime}Ry)\implies xRy

xRy\implies(\forall x^{\prime},x\leq_{\oplus}^{X}x^{\prime}\vee x^{\prime}Ry)\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad xRy\implies(\forall y^{\prime},xRy^{\prime}\vee y^{\prime}\leq_{\oplus}^{Y}y)

(ii)

Let
{\sf P_{max}}
-
Rel
be the linear quantaloid of sets and “extended” distance relations. Then consider
{\sf Mon}(\mbox{${\sf P_{max}}$-{\sf Rel}})
, a linear quantaloid of sets
X
endowed with a relation
m_{\otimes}\colon X\times X\rightarrow[0,\infty]
satisfying

m_{\otimes}(x,x)\leq 0\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad m_{\otimes}(x,x^{\prime\prime})\leq{\sf max}(m_{\otimes}(x,x^{\prime}),m_{\otimes}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}))

and relation
m_{\oplus}\colon X\times X\rightarrow[0,\infty]
satisfying

\infty\leq m_{\oplus}(x,x)\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad{\sf min}(m_{\oplus}(x,x^{\prime}),m_{\oplus}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}))\leq m_{\oplus}(x,x^{\prime\prime})

such that

{\sf min}(m_{\oplus}(x,x^{\prime}),m_{\otimes}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}))\leq m_{\otimes}(x,x^{\prime\prime})\quad\quad{\rm,}\quad\quad{\sf min}(m_{\otimes}(x,x^{\prime}),m_{\oplus}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}))\leq m_{\otimes}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})

m_{\oplus}(x,x^{\prime\prime})\leq{\sf max}(m_{\otimes}(x,x^{\prime}),m_{\oplus}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}))\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad m_{\oplus}(x,x^{\prime\prime})\leq{\sf max}(m_{\oplus}(x,x^{\prime}),m_{\otimes}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}))

These are Lawvere ultrametric spaces
(X,m_{\otimes})
with an additional distance relation
m_{\oplus}
which interact coherently with
m_{\otimes}
. The arrows
(X,m)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(Y,n)
are real-valued functions
f\colon X\times Y\rightarrow[0,\infty]
such that

f(x,y^{\prime})\leq{\sf max}(f(x,y),n_{\otimes}(y,y^{\prime})\quad\quad{\rm,}\quad\quad f(x,y)\leq{\sf max}(m_{\otimes}(x,x^{\prime}),f(x^{\prime},y))

{\sf min}(f(x,y^{\prime}),n_{\oplus}(y^{\prime},y))\leq f(x,y)\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad{\sf min}(m_{\otimes}(x,x^{\prime}),f(x^{\prime},y))\leq f(x,y)

We can of course do the same construction with
{\sf P_{+}}
-
Rel
and
[0,1]
-
Rel
and get similar examples of “linearized” metric spaces.

6
Non-locally posetal examples,
{\cal L}{\rm oc}
,
{\cal Q}{\rm uant}
and
{\cal Q}{\rm tld}

In this section, we present examples of linear bicategories which are not locally partially ordered.

While the bicategory of locales being a linear bicategory will be a consequence of Theorem
6.8
, we start with case of locales as it is the setting in which these greater results were first investigated.

Definition 6.1

(
[
18
]
)
.

Let
W,X
and
Y
be locales.

•

An
(X,Y)
-module
A\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y
is a sup lattice
A
which is a left
X
-module and a right
Y
-module satisfying
x(ay)=(xa)y
.

•

If
B
is a
(W,Y)
-module, then the sup lattice of right
Y
-module homomorphisms, denoted by
B~\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A
, becomes a
(W,X)
-module via
(wf)(a)=wf(a)
and
(fx)(a)=f(xa)
. Dually, if
C
is a left
(X,Z)
-module,
A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}~C
is a
(Y,Z)
-module.

•

A function
f\colon A\rightarrow A^{\prime}
is a module homomorphism if it is a left
X
-module and right
Y
-module homomorphism.

•

Suppose
A
is an
(X,Y)
-module. Then the opposite lattice
A^{\circ}
becomes a
(Y,X)
-module as follows. Given
x\in X
, the function
x\cdot-\colon A\rightarrow A
is sup-preserving map, and hence, has a right adjoint
-/x
. Thus,
A^{\circ}
becomes a right
X
-module via
(a,x)\mapsto a/x
and a left
Y
-module via
(y,a)\mapsto r\backslash a
, where
y\backslash-
is right adjoint to
-\cdot y
, and
(A^{\circ})^{\circ}\cong A
.

While not explicitly stated,
[
18
]
develops the required results to say that:

Theorem 6.2
.

There is a bicategory whose objects are locales, 1-cells are modules, and 2-cells are module homomorphisms. Composition of
A\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y
and
B\colon Y\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Z
is given by

A\otimes B=A\otimes_{Y}B\cong(B^{\circ}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A)^{\circ}\cong(B\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,A^{\circ})^{\circ}

with identity 1-cells
X\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}X
. We denote this bicategory by
{\cal L}{\rm oc}
.

Now, we note that
{\cal L}{\rm oc}
admits another composition of 1-cells

A\oplus B=(B^{\circ}\otimes A^{\circ})^{\circ}=(B^{\circ}\otimes_{Y}A^{\circ})^{\circ}\cong A^{\circ}\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,B

with identity 1-cells
X^{\circ}\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}X
, since
X^{\circ}\oplus A\cong A\cong A\oplus Y^{\circ}
and which is associative,

A\oplus(B\oplus C)=((C^{\circ}\otimes B^{\circ})\otimes A^{\circ})^{\circ}\cong(C^{\circ}\otimes(B^{\circ}\otimes A^{\circ}))^{\circ}=(A\oplus B)\oplus C

for all
C\colon Z\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}W
. To see that
{\cal L}{\rm oc}
is a linear bicategory, we will define the linear distributivity

A\otimes(B\oplus C)\rightarrow(A\otimes B)\oplus C

Since
A\otimes(B\oplus C)\cong A\otimes_{Y}(B^{\circ}\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,C)
and
(A\otimes B)\oplus C\cong(B^{\circ}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A)\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,C
, it suffices to define a 2-cell
A\otimes_{Y}(B^{\circ}\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,C)\rightarrow(B^{\circ}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A)\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,C
or equivalently,

(B^{\circ}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A)\otimes_{X}(A\otimes_{Y}(B^{\circ}\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,C))\rightarrow C

For this, we can use the evaluation maps

(B^{\circ}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A)\otimes_{X}(A\otimes_{Y}(B^{\circ}\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,C))\cong((B^{\circ}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A)\otimes_{X}A)\otimes_{Y}(B^{\circ}\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,C)\xrightarrow{\epsilon_{A,Y}}B^{\circ}\otimes_{Y}(B^{\circ}\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,C)\xrightarrow{\epsilon_{Z,B^{\circ}}}C

Similarly, we get a 2-cell
(A\oplus B)\otimes C\rightarrow A\oplus(B\otimes C)
, as desired.

Theorem 6.3
.

Under the above operations,
{\cal L}{\rm oc}
is a linear bicategory.

6.1
{\cal V}
-
Mat
,
Mon
{\cal B}
and
{\cal V}
-
{\cal P}\rm rof

Recall
{\cal V}
-
{\cal M}\rm at
, the bicategory of sets,
{\cal V}
-matrices and
{\cal V}
-matrix morphisms, which is biclosed when
{\cal V}
is a symmetric monoidal closed category with set-indexed products and coproduct by Lemma
2.17
. If
{\cal V}
is further
*
-autonomous,
{\cal V}
-
{\cal M}\rm at
becomes a linear bicategory, as stated in
[
9
]
:

Proposition 6.4
.

Consider
{\cal V}
-
{\cal M}\rm at
, where
{\cal V}
is a
*
-autonomous category with set-indexed products and coproducts. Given a set
X
, define
\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{X}\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}X
by

\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{X}(x,x^{\prime})=\begin{cases}\bot&x=x^{\prime}\\
\top&\text{otherwise}\\
\end{cases}

For
{\cal V}
-matrix
A\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}Y
, defining
A^{\perp}\colon Y\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}X
by
A^{\perp}(y,x)=A(x,y)^{\perp}=A(x,y)\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,\bot
, one can show that
(A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{Y})\cong A^{\perp}\cong(\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{X}\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A)
and
A\cong(A^{\perp})^{\perp}
, and so
{\cal V}
-
{\cal M}\rm at
is a cyclic
*
-autonomous bicategory with cyclic dualizing family
{\cal D}=\{\mbox{$\bot\!\!\!\bot$}_{X}\colon X\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}X\}
.

Now consider
Mon
{\cal B}
, the bicategory of monads in
{\cal B}
, modules and module morphisms, which is biclosed if
{\cal B}
is biclosed with local equalizers and coequalizers stable under composition by Lemma
2.19
. Then
Mon
{\cal B}
can be a linear bicategory:

Proposition 6.5
.

Suppose
{\cal B}
is a cyclic
*
-autonomous bicategory with local equalizers and coequalizers stable under composition, then
Mon
{\cal B}
is a cyclic
*
-autonomous bicategory.

Proof.

Suppose
\mbox{${\cal D}$\,\,}=\{{\bot_{X}}\colon X\rightarrow X\,|\,X\in\mbox{${\cal B}$}\}
is a cyclic dualizing family for
{\cal B}
. Define the family of modules

\mbox{${\cal D}$\,\,}^{\perp}=\{Q^{\perp}\colon(X,Q)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(X,Q)\,|\,(X,Q)\in\mbox{{\sf Mon}\mbox{${\cal B}$}}\}

This family is well-defined since the 1-cell
Q^{\perp}=Q\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,\bot_{X}\colon X\rightarrow X
is a module
(X,Q)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(X,Q)
with action
\lambda\colon Q\otimes Q^{\perp}\rightarrow Q^{\perp}
defined by the transpose of

Q\otimes(Q\otimes Q^{\perp})\xrightarrow{\sim}(Q\otimes Q)\otimes Q^{\perp}\xrightarrow{m\otimes Q^{\perp}}Q\otimes Q^{\perp}\xrightarrow{\epsilon_{X,Q}}\bot_{X}

and action
\rho
defined as
Q^{\perp}\otimes Q\xrightarrow{\sim}(\bot_{X}\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}Q)\otimes Q\xrightarrow{\rho^{\prime}}\bot_{X}\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}Q\xrightarrow{\sim}Q^{\perp}
, where
\rho^{\prime}
is the tranpose of

((\bot_{X}\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}Q)\otimes Q)\otimes Q\xrightarrow{\sim}(\bot_{X}\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}Q)\otimes(Q\otimes Q)\xrightarrow{(\bot_{X}\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}Q)\otimes m}(\bot_{X}\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}Q)\otimes Q\xrightarrow{\epsilon_{Q,X}}\bot_{X}

Given a monad module
A\colon(X,Q)\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}(Y,R)
, applying Proposition
2.14
, we see that

A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}_{Q}\,Q^{\perp}=A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}_{Q}(Q\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\bot_{X})=A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}_{Q}(Q\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}_{\top_{X}}\bot_{X})\cong A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}_{\top_{X}}\bot_{X}=A\mbox{$-\!\!\!\!\!-\!\!\circ$}\,\bot_{X}=A^{\perp}

and

R^{\perp}\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}_{R}A\cong(\bot_{Y}\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}R)\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}_{R}A=(\bot_{Y}\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}_{\top_{Y}}R)\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}_{R}A\cong\bot_{Y}\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}_{\top_{Y}}A=\bot_{Y}\,\mbox{$\circ\!\!-\!\!\!\!\!-$}A\cong A^{\perp}

Since all these 2-cells are natural in
A
,
\mbox{${\cal D}$\,\,}^{\perp}
is a cyclic dualizing family for
Mon
{\cal B}
.
∎

Finally, recalling that
{\cal V}
-
{\cal P}\rm rof
can be constructed as
{\sf Mon}({\cal V}
-
{\cal M}\rm at)
by Proposition
2.20
, and by the above two results:

Proposition 6.6
.

If
{\cal V}
is a complete and cocomplete
*
-autonomous category, then
{\cal V}
-
{\cal P}\rm rof
is a cyclic
*
-autonomous bicategory.

6.2
Linear bicategories
{\cal Q}{\rm uant}
and
{\cal Q}{\rm tld}

Joyal and Tierney observed that for a fixed commutative unital quantale
Q
, the category of right
Q
-modules and module homomorphisms is a
*
-autonomous category
[
18
]
. Rosenthal expanded this result to show that the category of
(Q,Q)
-modules is equally
*
-autonomous and that, given a small quantaloid
{\cal Q}
, the category of
(\mbox{${\cal Q}$},\mbox{${\cal Q}$})
-modules is
*
-autonomous
[
24
]
.

Now that we have access to the notion of linear bicategories,
Q
and
{\cal Q}
no longer need to be fixed and we can claim the following examples of cyclic
*
-autonomous bicategories.

Definition 6.7
.

•

Given unital quantales
Q
and
R
, a
(Q,R)
-module
Q\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}R
is a suplattice
A
which is a left
Q
-module and a right
R
-module, i.e., there are suplattice homomorphisms
\star\colon Q\times A\rightarrow A
and
\cdot\colon A\times R\rightarrow A
such that

(q\otimes q^{\prime})\star a=q\star(q^{\prime}\star a)\quad,\quad a\cdot(r\otimes r^{\prime})=(a\cdot r)\cdot r^{\prime}

\top\star a=a\quad,\quad a\cdot\top=a\quad{\rm and}\quad(q\star a)\cdot r=q\star(a\cdot r)

•

Given
(Q,R)
-modules
A
and
B
, a module homomorphism is a suplattice homomorphism
f\colon A\rightarrow B
such that

f(q\star a)=q\star f(a)\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad f(a\cdot r)=f(a)\cdot r

•

Let
{\cal Q}{\rm uant}
denote the bicategory of unital quantales, modules and module morphisms. Then,
{\cal Q}{\rm uant}
is
{\sf Mon}{\cal B}({\rm Sup})
, the bicategory of monoids, monoid modules and module morphisms in
{\rm Sup}
.

If
{\cal V}
is
*
-autonomous category with equalizers and coequalizers, its suspension
\cal B(V)
is a cyclic
*
-autonomous bicategory with local equalizers and co-equalizers stable under composition. Then, by Proposition
6.5
, the bicategory
{\sf Mon}(\cal B(V))
is a cyclic
*
-autonomous bicategory. Taking
\mbox{${\cal V}$}={\rm Sup}
, we get:

Theorem 6.8
.

The bicategory
{\cal Q}{\rm uant}
is a cyclic
*
-autonomous bicategory.

Definition 6.9
.

•

Given small quantaloids
{\cal Q}
and
{\cal R}
, a
(\mbox{${\cal Q}$},{\cal R})
-module
A\colon\mbox{${\cal Q}$}\hbox{$\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>$\hskip-14.45377pt\raisebox{1.3pt}{\tiny$\bullet$} \hskip 7.22743pt}{\cal R}
consists of, for each
q,q^{\prime}\in{\rm ob}\mbox{${\cal Q}$},r,r^{\prime}\in{\rm ob}{\cal R}
:

–

a suplattice
A(q,r)
,

–

a left action suplattice homomoprhism
\star\colon\mbox{${\cal Q}$}(q,q^{\prime})\times A(q^{\prime},r)\rightarrow A(q,r)
such that given
a\in A(q,r)

\top_{q}\star a=a\quad{\rm for}\quad q=q^{\prime}\quad\quad(f\otimes g)\star a=f\star(g\star a)\quad{\rm for}\quad f\colon q\rightarrow q^{\prime},g\colon q^{\prime}\rightarrow q^{\prime\prime}

–

a right action suplattice homomoprhism
\cdot\colon A(q,r)\times{\cal R}(r,r^{\prime})\rightarrow A(q,r^{\prime})
such that given
a\in A(q,r)

a\cdot\top_{r}=a\quad{\rm for}\quad r=r^{\prime}\quad\quad a\cdot(h\otimes k)=(a\cdot h)\cdot k\quad{\rm for}\quad h\colon r\rightarrow r^{\prime},k\colon r^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime\prime}

satisfying
\forall a\in A(q,r),f\colon q\rightarrow q^{\prime}\in\mbox{${\cal Q}$},h\colon r\rightarrow r^{\prime}\in{\cal R}
,

(f\star a)\cdot h=f\star(a\cdot h)

•

Given
(\mbox{${\cal Q}$},{\cal R})
-modules
A
and
B
, a module homomorphism
f\colon A\rightarrow B
is a family of suplattice homomorphisms
f_{q,r}\colon A(q,r)\rightarrow B(q,r)
satisfying, for
f\colon q\rightarrow q^{\prime}\in\mbox{${\cal Q}$},a^{\prime}\in A(q^{\prime},r),a\in A(q,r),h\colon r\rightarrow r^{\prime}\in{\cal R}
,

f_{q,r}(f\star a^{\prime})=q\star f_{q^{\prime},r}(a^{\prime})\quad\quad{\rm and}\quad\quad f_{q,r^{\prime}}(a\cdot h)=f_{q,r}(a)\cdot h

•

Ler
{\cal Q}{\rm tld}
denote the bicategory of small quantaloids, modules and module homomorphisms. Then,
{\cal Q}{\rm tld}
is
{\rm Sup}
-
{\cal P}\rm rof
, the bicategory of
{\rm Sup}
-categories,
{\rm Sup}
-profunctors and
{\rm Sup}
-transformations.

By proposition
6.6
and taking
\mbox{${\cal V}$}={\rm Sup}
, we get:

Theorem 6.10
.

The bicategory
{\cal Q}{\rm tld}
is a cyclic
*
-autonomous bicategory.
